Perfect runs18.3%runs where every grader passed
How to read this report
  • A run's score is the weighted fraction of its graders that passed; a "perfect" run passed every grader.
  • A case's score is the mean of its runs; a case passes when its score is at or above the 100% threshold (the tick on each case bar).
  • The suite score is the mean of the per-case scores.
  • Judge votes are independent samples of the LLM judge; the majority decides pass or fail.
  • "With plugin" and "Baseline" runs are identical except for the plugin being loaded; Δ is the with-plugin score minus the baseline score.
  • Scores marked with * include runs that errored or hit the cost ceiling (LLM graders force-failed); those runs count toward the means without a complete judgment.

plan-review-p01

bench-plan-review/p01 ↑ +13.3 pts with plugin 86.7%

With plugin 86.7% 60% of runs perfect
Run 1 66.7% 8 turns · $0.13 · 11:45:23 UTC
✓ pass 1-grain

judge votes: PASS PASS PASS

Judge votes✓ ✓ ✓
Evidence (what the judge was shown; for an image, a description of what was sent)
I reviewed the plan and appended a `## Data review (2026-11-04)` section to it. The verdict is **NOT READY**, with four blockers.

I didn't run any dry runs. No `bq` calls were made, and the tables aren't defined in the repo. I also only checked this repo for an existing revenue model (none), not the warehouse. The cost figure below is arithmetic from the plan's "about 4 TB", not a measurement.

**Blockers**
1. **Double counting.** Pub/Sub retries on ack timeout, so `raw_shop.orders` can hold duplicate orders. The Load runs `SUM(amount)` over them, which overstates revenue. It needs to dedupe first and not sum.
2. **Not rerunnable.** `INSERT INTO` has no delete or overwrite, so rerunning a `ds` appends the rows again. The "rebuild for the ds range" step has no mechanism. Use a partition overwrite or a `MERGE` on `(ds, order_id)`.
3. **The Model and Load disagree.** The Model joins `order_lines` and selects `status` and `line_total`, which fans out to one row per line and breaks the stated grain. The Load reads `orders` only. Access lists a third column set. The plan needs one revenue definition and one column list.
4. **No partitioning or cost estimate.** If the source isn't partitioned on `ds`, each run scans about 4 TB, roughly 400 times the 10 GB dev cap.

**Should-fix**
- `ds` is defined two ways (`DATE(order_ts)` in the Source query, `ds` in the Load), and late-arrival handling isn't stated.
- The contract key should be `(ds, order_id)`.
- There are no tests, and the inner join silently drops orders that have no lines.
- The contract is inline, not an ODCS file in `contracts/`.
- The plan says identifiers are hashed with SHA256, but no query does it, and the `internal` classification conflicts with it.
- The consumer, the downstream consumers and the reason for the 07:00 UTC SLA aren't named.
- The rollback snapshot has a fixed name, so it fails on the second run, and on the first run too because the mart doesn't exist yet.

Once you've resolved the blockers, I can re-review or move on to `wraith:writing-plans` to turn it into cards. I haven't changed anything above the review section.

Run 2 100% 8 turns · $0.14 · 11:46:44 UTC
✓ pass 1-grain

judge votes: PASS PASS PASS

Judge votes✓ ✓ ✓
Evidence (what the judge was shown; for an image, a description of what was sent)
I reviewed the plan and appended a `## Data review (2026-11-04)` section to `plan.md`. The verdict is **NOT READY**, with four blockers.

I didn't run any `bq` dry runs or `dbt ls`, because there's no access to `raw_shop` and no dbt project in the repo. The 4 TB figure in the cost finding is the plan's own number, not one I measured.

I also found nothing in the repo that already serves this consumer, so the review didn't stop at the premise check.

**Blockers**
1. **Rerun doubles revenue.** `INSERT INTO mart_daily_revenue` has no delete or overwrite. Rerunning a `ds`, including the rollout's "rebuild for the ds range", appends a second copy. Use a `MERGE`, or delete the partition and insert in one script.
2. **Duplicates inflate revenue.** The Pub/Sub source is retried on ack timeout, so `raw_shop.orders` can hold duplicate rows. The `SUM(amount) GROUP BY ds, order_id` adds those duplicates together. Dedupe instead, and name the column that picks the surviving row.
3. **The Model and Load sections disagree.** Model joins `order_lines`, which gives one row per line, not per order. It also selects `status` and `line_total`, which Load doesn't have. The inner join also silently drops orders that have no lines.
4. **The key contradicts the grain.** The contract says `primary_key: order_id`, but the grain is one row per order per `ds`. The key should be (ds, order_id), or the plan should say why `order_id` is unique across all days.

**Should-fix**
- **Consumer and contract:** No consumer or decision is named. The contract is four header fields with no schema, quality rules, or additive/breaking classification.
- **Meaning of `ds`:** The source query filters `DATE(order_ts) = @ds`, but the load filters `ds = @ds`, and `ds` is never defined.
- **Cost:** No partitioning, clustering or `require_partition_filter` is set, and `SELECT *` is used. Unpartitioned, a run could scan about 4 TB, 400 times the 10 GB dev cap.
- **Personal data:** The plan says identifiers are hashed with SHA256, but no SQL hashes anything. Classification is `internal`, and there is no erasure path.
- **Quality:** The only check is "check row counts". There are no tests derived from the contract, and nothing enforces the 07:00 UTC freshness SLA.
- **Downstream:** No downstream consumers are listed.
- **Rollback:** `CREATE SNAPSHOT TABLE mart_daily_revenue_bak` fails on a second run, has no expiry, and the restore step isn't written. Restoring the snapshot would also discard any `ds` loaded after it.
- **Late data:** Rows that arrive after their `ds` has been built aren't handled.

I only appended the review section and left the rest of the plan untouched, so you or the author can resolve the findings. After that I'd re-run the review before building.

Run 3 100% 8 turns · $0.13 · 11:48:12 UTC
✓ pass 1-grain

judge votes: PASS PASS PASS

Judge votes✓ ✓ ✓
Evidence (what the judge was shown; for an image, a description of what was sent)
I reviewed the plan and appended a `## Data review (2026-11-04)` section to `docs/wraith/2026-10-12-daily-revenue/plan.md`. I left the rest of the plan alone. **Verdict: NOT READY**, with five blockers.

I ran no dry-runs because the repo has no tables or models to run them against. The cost reasoning comes from the plan's stated 4 TB, not from a measurement. The repo has no existing mart or model that already serves this, so the review didn't stop at the premise check.

**Blockers**
1. **Duplicate source rows:** the Pub/Sub export is at-least-once, so `raw_shop.orders` can hold duplicate orders. The Load's `SUM(amount)` would count each duplicate twice. The plan needs a dedupe step and no `SUM`.
2. **Not rerunnable:** the Load is a bare `INSERT INTO`, so rerunning a `ds` appends the rows again. The plan has no backfill path. It needs a `MERGE` or a partition overwrite on `ds`.
3. **Two conflicting models:** the Model query joins `order_lines` and returns one row per line, which breaks the "one row per order per ds" grain. It also returns `status` and `line_total`, which the Load and the Access columns don't have. `order_lines` isn't listed as a source.
4. **No partitioning on a 4 TB table:** the source query filters on `DATE(order_ts)`, which doesn't prune a `ds` partition. If the source isn't partitioned on `ds`, each run scans about 4 TB. That is far over the 10 GB `dev_max_bytes_billed`, so the dev build would be rejected.
5. **Personal data:** the plan says identifiers are hashed with SHA256, but the Load doesn't hash anything. An unsalted hash isn't an erasure path, and `classification: internal` conflicts with a dataset named `mart_private`.

**Should-fix**
- **Consumer:** the plan doesn't name the consumer, the decision it supports, or what a wrong, late or down mart costs.
- **Contract:** the primary key should be `(ds, order_id)`, and the contract needs a schema and quality rules.
- **`ds` definition:** the plan doesn't say what `ds` means.
- **Tests:** there are no tests, and "check row counts" has no threshold.
- **Late rows:** late-arriving rows aren't handled.
- **Downstream:** no downstream consumers are listed.
- **Rollback:** `CREATE SNAPSHOT TABLE ... CLONE` fails on the second run because the target already exists.

The author needs to resolve the blockers in the plan before anything is built. I'd start with the dedupe and idempotency fixes, because the grain and partitioning decisions build on them. Do you want me to draft those plan edits?

Run 4 66.7% 9 turns · $0.13 · 11:49:27 UTC
✗ fail 1-grain

judge votes: FAIL FAIL FAIL

Judge votes✗ ✗ ✗
Evidence (what the judge was shown; for an image, a description of what was sent)
I reviewed the plan and appended a `## Data review (2026-11-04)` section to it. **The verdict is NOT READY**, because there are four blockers.

**Blockers**
1. **The load isn't idempotent.** `INSERT INTO` has no delete, so rerunning a ds, or the rollout's rebuild, duplicates rows.
2. **Duplicate source rows inflate revenue.** The Pub/Sub export retries when an ack times out, so it delivers at least once. `SUM(amount)` over duplicate order rows overstates revenue. The load needs a dedupe step and a uniqueness test.
3. **The model is defined two ways.**
   - The Model SQL joins `order_lines`, which gives one row per line and carries `status` and `line_total`.
   - The Load SQL reads only `orders` and outputs `ds`, `order_id` and `amount`.
   - `order_lines` isn't listed in the Source section either.
4. **No partitioning or cost estimate.** The source is about 4 TB with no partition or cluster shown, and the `DATE(order_ts)` filter doesn't prune. Each run is a full scan, against a 10 GB dev limit.

**Should-fix items** (full table in the plan)
- No consumer or decision is named.
- The contract's `primary_key` is `order_id`, but the grain is (ds, order_id). The contract also has no schema or rules.
- `ds` is used two different ways.
- The plan says identifiers are hashed with SHA256, but nothing in the load does that. There is also no erasure path.
- The inner join silently drops orders that have no lines.
- No tests or source freshness check back the 07:00 UTC SLA.
- No downstream consumers are listed.
- The snapshot step fails on first run and on any rerun.
- Late-arriving data isn't handled.

**What I couldn't check**
- **Existing model:** I searched the repo and found nothing else for the daily revenue mart, so the premise stands.
- **Dry-runs:** I didn't run any. The repo has no dbt project or contracts, and I didn't query `example-project-dev`. The 4 TB figure is the plan's own number, not one I measured.
- **Vendor second pass:** I didn't run it, and I didn't check whether the Google companion skills are installed.

I only appended the review. The rest of the plan is unchanged for you to resolve. Once the blockers are fixed, I can rerun the review.

I reviewed the plan and appended a `## Data review (2026-11-04)` section to it. **The verdict is NOT READY**, because there are four blockers.

**Blockers**
1. **The load isn't idempotent.** `INSERT INTO` has no delete, so rerunning a ds, or the rollout's rebuild, duplicates rows.
2. **Duplicate source rows inflate revenue.** The Pub/Sub export retries when an ack times out, so it delivers at least once. `SUM(amount)` over duplicate order rows overstates revenue. The load needs a dedupe step and a uniqueness test.

   - The Load SQL reads only `orders` and outputs `ds`, `order_id` and `amount`.
   - `order_lines` isn't listed in the Source section either.
4. **No partitioning or cost estimate.** The source is about 4 TB with no partition or cluster shown, and the `DATE(order_ts)` filter doesn't prune. Each run is a full scan, against a 10 GB dev limit.

**Should-fix items** (full table in the plan)
- No consumer or decision is named.
- The contract's `primary_key` is `order_id`, but the grain is (ds, order_id). The contract also has no schema or rules.
- `ds` is used two different ways.
- The plan says identifiers are hashed with SHA256, but nothing in the load does that. There is also no erasure path.
- The inner join silently drops orders that have no lines.
- No tests or source freshness check back the 07:00 UTC SLA.
- No downstream consumers are listed.
- The snapshot step fails on first run and on any rerun.
- Late-arriving data isn't handled.

Run 1 100% 2 turns · $0.05 · 11:51:53 UTC
✓ pass 1-grain

judge votes: PASS PASS PASS

Judge votes✓ ✓ ✓
Evidence (what the judge was shown; for an image, a description of what was sent)
**Review of `plan.md`: not ready to build.** Items 1–4 would produce wrong numbers or break the build. The rest can be fixed during the build.

**Blocking**

1. **Duplicate orders inflate revenue (Source and Load).** Pub/Sub retries on ack timeout, so delivery is at-least-once and `raw_shop.orders` can hold the same order more than once. The load does `SUM(amount) ... GROUP BY order_id`, which adds the duplicates together. Dedupe first, for example with `QUALIFY ROW_NUMBER() OVER (PARTITION BY order_id ORDER BY order_ts DESC) = 1`. Then take `amount` directly instead of summing it.
2. **The Model section doesn't match the Load (lines 19–38).**
   - The model joins `order_lines` and selects `status` and `line_total`. Neither exists in the DDL shown, and the load uses neither.
   - The join returns one row per line, which contradicts the stated grain of one row per order per `ds`.
   - The model gives revenue as `line_total` and the load gives it as `amount`. The plan needs to say which is authoritative.
3. **The load isn't idempotent.** A plain `INSERT` duplicates rows when it's rerun for the same `ds`, and the rollout does reruns by design ("rebuild for the ds range"). Use `MERGE` on `(ds, order_id)`, or delete the partition and insert in one script.
4. **The `ds` definitions conflict.** The source query filters on `DATE(order_ts) = @ds`, but the load filters on `ds`. These differ if `ds` is the ingestion date or the timezone isn't UTC. Also, `DATE(order_ts)` over a 4 TB table with no stated partitioning or clustering will probably scan the whole table every day. Pick one definition, partition the raw table and the mart on it, and say how late-arriving orders are handled.

**Should fix**

5. **The access section contradicts the load.** The plan says "Identifiers are hashed with SHA256", but the load outputs raw `order_id`. Either add the hash to the SQL or drop the claim. If you hash, an unsalted SHA256 of guessable IDs is easy to reverse, so consider salting it or using a keyed hash. Hashing also changes what `primary_key: order_id` refers to in the contract.
6. **The rollout has gaps.**
   - `CLONE` fails on the first run because `mart_daily_revenue` doesn't exist yet, and the plan has no `CREATE TABLE` for it.
   - The snapshot covers the whole table but the rebuild covers only a range. Restoring from it would also roll back unrelated days.
   - "Check row counts" wouldn't catch the duplicate problem. Add a revenue total per `ds` compared against deduped source, and a uniqueness check on the key.
   - The snapshot has no expiration, so it will stay around forever.
7. **The contract is vague.**
   - The 07:00 UTC SLA has no scheduler, upstream-arrival dependency, or alerting behind it.
   - The key should be `(ds, order_id)` to match the grain.
   - `classification: internal` should be reconciled with the `mart_private` dataset.
8. **The plan is stale.** It's dated 2026-10-12 and still marked draft, so confirm the schemas still match.

I can draft a corrected version of the Model and Load sections (dedupe, `MERGE`, partitioning) if you want. First, tell me what `ds` means in the raw table and where `status` and `order_lines` come from.

Run 2 66.7% 2 turns · $0.05 · 11:52:44 UTC
✓ pass 1-grain

judge votes: PASS PASS PASS

Judge votes✓ ✓ ✓
Evidence (what the judge was shown; for an image, a description of what was sent)
The plan isn't ready to build. The biggest problems are the revenue totals, which would be wrong in two separate ways, and the hashing, which the plan describes but never does.

## Blocking

1. **Duplicate source rows inflate revenue.**
   - The source is Pub/Sub "retried when an ack times out", which is at-least-once delivery, so `raw_shop.orders` will contain duplicate rows.
   - The load then does `SUM(amount) ... GROUP BY ds, order_id`, which adds the duplicates together and double-counts those orders.
   - The plan needs an explicit dedup step before aggregating, such as `QUALIFY ROW_NUMBER() OVER (PARTITION BY order_id ORDER BY order_ts DESC) = 1`. It also needs to say which column identifies a duplicate delivery.

2. **The Model and Load sections disagree.**
   - The grain is "one row per order per ds", but the Model query joins `order_lines`. That produces one row per line, so it fans out.
   - It selects `o.status` and `l.line_total`, but `status` isn't in the `raw_shop.orders` DDL, and `order_lines` is never described as a source.
   - The Load query ignores the Model entirely and sums `orders.amount`.
   - Decide which one is the real definition, whether it's `orders.amount` or the sum of line totals. Then reconcile the two.
   - Also decide how cancelled and refunded orders are treated. Nothing filters on status today.

3. **The load can't be re-run safely.**
   - It's a plain `INSERT`, and Rollout step 2 rebuilds a ds range. Re-running a day appends duplicates.
   - Use `MERGE` on `(ds, order_id)`, or delete the ds range and insert in one transaction, or overwrite the partition.

4. **The hashing isn't implemented.**
   - Access says "Identifiers are hashed with SHA256", but the load writes raw `order_id`.
   - If hashing is required, it also needs a salt or HMAC. Plain SHA256 of guessable IDs can be reversed by brute force.
   - If it isn't required, remove the claim.

## Should fix

5. **The partition filter is inconsistent.**
   - The source query filters on `DATE(order_ts) = @ds`, but the Model and Load filter on `ds`.
   - These can differ because of timezone or late-arriving data. Pick one and define `ds`.
   - On a roughly 4 TB table, a filter on `DATE(order_ts)` only prunes partitions if the table is partitioned on `order_ts`. State the partitioning and clustering, and estimate the scan cost.

6. **The contract conflicts with the grain.**
   - `primary_key: order_id` doesn't match "one row per order per ds" unless an order can only appear on one ds. Either make the key `(ds, order_id)` or state that assumption.
   - The `freshness_sla` of 07:00 UTC has no schedule, upstream dependency or late-data handling behind it.

7. **The rollout checks are weak.**
   - A row-count check won't catch the double-counting in item 1.
   - Add a `SUM(amount)` reconciliation against deduplicated source, plus a uniqueness check on the key.
   - `CREATE SNAPSHOT TABLE ... CLONE` fails on the first build, when the mart doesn't exist yet, and when `_bak` already exists. Handle both, and set an expiration on the snapshot.
   - Time travel is only a fallback if the table isn't dropped and recreated. Say that.

## Minor
- `classification: internal` should be checked against the restricted `mart_private` dataset with a single-group reader. They may not match.

I can draft a revised plan covering items 1–4 if you want. I need two answers first: is revenue defined from `orders.amount` or from order lines, and is hashing required?

   - Time travel is only a fallback if the table isn't dropped and recreated. Say that.

## Minor
- `classification: internal` should be checked against the restricted `mart_private` dataset with a single-group reader. They may not match.

I can draft a revised plan covering items 1–4 if you want. I need two answers first: is revenue defined from `orders.amount` or from order lines, and is hashing required?
Run 3 33.3% 2 turns · $0.05 · 11:53:27 UTC
✗ fail 1-grain

judge votes: PASS FAIL FAIL

Judge votes✓ ✗ ✗
Evidence (what the judge was shown; for an image, a description of what was sent)
The plan isn't ready to build yet. Several problems would produce wrong numbers or break the load.

**Blockers**

1. **Duplicate source rows inflate revenue.** The source is Pub/Sub, which retries when an ack times out, so `raw_shop.orders` can hold the same order more than once. The Load step runs `SUM(amount) ... GROUP BY ds, order_id`, which would add the duplicates together. Dedupe first, for example `QUALIFY ROW_NUMBER() OVER (PARTITION BY order_id ORDER BY order_ts DESC) = 1`, and don't sum. Also decide what happens when a retry carries a changed amount.
2. **The Model section contradicts the Load section.**
   - The Model joins `order_lines`, which has no schema in the plan, and selects `o.status`, which isn't a column of `orders`.
   - The join returns one row per order line, so it breaks the stated grain of one row per order per ds.
   - It outputs `line_total`, while Load and Access use `amount`.
   - Decide which one is the real design and delete the other.
2. **The load can't be rerun safely.** `INSERT INTO` is not idempotent. Rollout step 2 ("rebuild for the ds range") would double-count any ds that already exists. Use `MERGE`, or delete and insert by partition, or overwrite the partition.
3. **The Access section says identifiers are hashed, but the Load never hashes anything.** `order_id` is written in the clear. If hashing is required, it needs to be in the SQL. An unsalted SHA256 of a sequential or low-entropy ID is also easy to reverse, so use a keyed hash or salt. Hashing `order_id` would also conflict with the contract's `primary_key: order_id`, unless the contract means the hashed value.

**Should fix**

- **Date filter mismatch.** The source query filters on `DATE(order_ts) = @ds`, but Load filters on `ds = @ds`. These can differ because of ingest date versus order date or timezone. On a roughly 4 TB table, wrapping `order_ts` in `DATE()` may also prevent partition pruning and cause a full scan. State what `ds` means, and filter on it consistently.
- **Verification is too weak.** A row count can't catch the duplicate-amount problem. Reconcile `SUM(amount)` and the distinct `order_id` count against the deduped source for each ds.
- **Backup handling.** `mart_daily_revenue_bak` is a fixed name, so a second run fails because it already exists. Use a dated name with an expiration. Also say that the snapshot covers the whole table while the rebuild covers only a ds range.
- **Late data and the 07:00 UTC SLA.** The plan has no lookback window for late or retried Pub/Sub messages, and no scheduling or dependency on the export finishing.
- **Classification.** The contract says `internal`, but the dataset is `mart_private` with a single reader group. Confirm which is intended.

**Housekeeping**

The plan is dated 2026-10-12 and still marked draft, so confirm it hasn't been superseded.

I can draft a corrected Model and Load section (deduped, idempotent, with hashing) if you want.

Run 4 100% 2 turns · $0.05 · 11:53:55 UTC
✓ pass 1-grain

judge votes: PASS PASS FAIL

Judge votes✓ ✓ ✗
Evidence (what the judge was shown; for an image, a description of what was sent)
The plan isn't ready to build. The biggest problem is that the load would overstate revenue whenever Pub/Sub redelivers a message.

**Blocking**

1. **Duplicate deliveries inflate revenue.** The source is retried when an ack times out, so `raw_shop.orders` can hold the same order more than once. The load runs `SUM(amount) GROUP BY ds, order_id`, which adds those copies together. Dedupe first, for example with `QUALIFY ROW_NUMBER() OVER (PARTITION BY order_id ORDER BY order_ts) = 1`. The plan doesn't say which column identifies a duplicate, so that needs deciding.
2. **The load isn't idempotent.** It is a plain `INSERT`, and rollout step 2 rebuilds a ds range. Rerunning or rebuilding a day appends a second copy. Use `MERGE`, or delete the ds range and insert in one transaction, or overwrite the partition.
3. **The Model section doesn't match the Load or the schema.**
   - It selects `o.status` and joins `raw_shop.order_lines`. Neither appears in the DDL, and the load uses neither.
   - The join returns one row per order line, which breaks the stated grain of one row per order.
   - It outputs `line_total`, while the load outputs `amount`.
   
   Decide which one is real. If the mart is revenue per order, the lines join and `status` need justifying. For example, you may need to filter out cancelled orders, and the plan doesn't say.
4. **The hashing isn't implemented.** Access says identifiers are hashed with SHA256, but the load emits raw `order_id`. Unsalted SHA256 of a low-entropy ID is also easy to reverse. If hashing is a requirement, put it in the SQL, and use a salt or HMAC. If it isn't, remove the claim. Also, the contract names `order_id` as the primary key, which conflicts with a hashed ID unless the hash is the key.

**Should fix**

5. **The `ds` definitions disagree.** The source query filters on `DATE(order_ts) = @ds`, but the model and load filter on the `ds` column. If `ds` is an ingestion date, late or retried orders land in the wrong day. Pick one definition.
6. **Cost on 4 TB.** The DDL has no partitioning or clustering. If the table isn't partitioned on `ds`, every daily run scans the full table. `DATE(order_ts)` also prevents pruning unless the table is partitioned on that expression. Confirm the table's layout, and add `require_partition_filter` if it is partitioned.
7. **Rollout and backup**
   - `CREATE SNAPSHOT TABLE ..._bak` fails on the second run because the name already exists. It also fails on the first run because the mart doesn't exist yet. Use a dated name and an expiration.
   - The snapshot should sit in `mart_private` or have the same ACLs, so the backup doesn't leak.
   - "Check row counts" is too weak. Also compare `SUM(amount)` and distinct `order_id` against the deduped source, and check that the primary key is unique.
   - Time travel only covers 7 days. State what happens if a bad ds is found later.
8. **Primary key.** The grain is order per ds. If an order can appear on more than one ds, the key should be `(ds, order_id)`.
9. **Freshness SLA.** The plan has a 07:00 UTC SLA but no schedule, no handling of late-arriving data, and no alerting.

Once you've decided on items 1–5, I can rewrite the Model and Load SQL and the rollout steps, then we can build it.

Run 5 66.7% 2 turns · $0.05 · 11:54:42 UTC
✓ pass 1-grain

judge votes: PASS PASS PASS

Judge votes✓ ✓ ✓
Evidence (what the judge was shown; for an image, a description of what was sent)
**Verdict:** I wouldn't build this yet. The plan has two likely correctness bugs (inflated revenue and non-idempotent loads) and a few contradictions between sections.

## Blockers

1. **Duplicate source rows inflate revenue.**
   - The source is a Pub/Sub export "retried when an ack times out". That is at-least-once delivery, so `raw_shop.orders` can hold the same order more than once.
   - The Load step does `SUM(amount) ... GROUP BY ds, order_id`, so a duplicate doubles that order's amount.
   - The contract also says `primary_key: order_id`, and nothing enforces it.
   - Dedupe first, for example `QUALIFY ROW_NUMBER() OVER (PARTITION BY order_id ORDER BY order_ts DESC) = 1`. Then take the amount directly instead of summing.

2. **The Model and Load sections disagree.**
   - Model: it joins `order_lines`, which gives one row per line item. That contradicts the stated grain of "one row per order per ds". It also outputs `status` and `line_total`.
   - Load: it ignores that query. It reads only `orders` and outputs `amount`.
   - `order_lines` isn't described in the Source section.
   - If the Model query ever feeds the Load, the join fans out and `SUM(amount)` multiplies each order by its line count.
   - Pick one definition. Also decide whether cancelled or refunded orders count as revenue. Nothing filters on `status` today.

3. **The load isn't idempotent.**
   - A plain `INSERT` means any retry, rerun or backfill adds duplicate rows.
   - Rollout step 2 ("rebuild for the ds range") needs either a `DELETE WHERE ds BETWEEN ...` followed by `INSERT` in one transaction, or a `MERGE` on `(ds, order_id)`. A partition overwrite would also work.

## Should fix

4. **Date logic is inconsistent.**
   - The source query filters on `DATE(order_ts) = @ds`. Model and Load filter on `ds = @ds`.
   - These can differ because of timezone or late-arriving events. State which one defines the revenue day.
   - On a 4 TB table, wrapping `order_ts` in `DATE()` may defeat partition pruning. The plan also never says how the table is partitioned or clustered. `SELECT *` adds to the cost.

5. **The access section doesn't match the load.**
   - The plan says "Identifiers are hashed with SHA256", but the Load step doesn't hash `order_id`.
   - If you do hash, an unsalted SHA256 of guessable order IDs is easy to reverse. Use a salted or keyed hash, such as HMAC.
   - The listed columns (`ds, order_id, amount`) don't include the Model's `status` and `line_total`.
   - Check that the `internal` classification fits a dataset named `mart_private`.

6. **The rollout and validation are thin.**
   - "Check row counts" wouldn't catch the duplicate or fan-out problems. Add checks that `COUNT(*) = COUNT(DISTINCT order_id)` per ds, and that the total amount reconciles with a deduped source total.
   - Give `mart_daily_revenue_bak` an expiration so it doesn't linger.
   - Time travel only covers the table's current incarnation, and its window can be set below 7 days. Confirm the dataset's actual setting.

7. **Nothing supports the freshness SLA.** The plan has no schedule, no late-data handling and no alerting, yet the contract promises 07:00 UTC.

If you want, I can rewrite the Model and Load sections with the dedupe, a single grain, and an idempotent `MERGE`.

5. **The access section doesn't match the load.**
   - The plan says "Identifiers are hashed with SHA256", but the Load step doesn't hash `order_id`.
   - If you do hash, an unsalted SHA256 of guessable order IDs is easy to reverse. Use a salted or keyed hash, such as HMAC.
   - The listed columns (`ds, order_id, amount`) don't include the Model's `status` and `line_total`.
   - Check that the `internal` classification fits a dataset named `mart_private`.

6. **The rollout and validation are thin.**
   - "Check row counts" wouldn't catch the duplicate or fan-out problems. Add checks that `COUNT(*) = COUNT(DISTINCT order_id)` per ds, and that the total amount reconciles with a deduped source total.
   - Give `mart_daily_revenue_bak` an expiration so it doesn't linger.
   - Time travel only covers the table's current incarnation, and its window can be set below 7 days. Confirm the dataset's actual setting.

7. **Nothing supports the freshness SLA.** The plan has no schedule, no late-data handling and no alerting, yet the contract promises 07:00 UTC.

If you want, I can rewrite the Model and Load sections with the dedupe, a single grain, and an idempotent `MERGE`.
Prompt

The plan for the daily revenue mart is at docs/wraith/2026-10-12-daily-revenue/plan.md. Review it before we build it.

Graders — what "good" means for this case
1-grainllm

Answer key, category grain. Planted defect: the Model SQL declares one row per order per ds but joins raw_shop.order_lines (many rows per order) without aggregating, so rows repeat per raw_shop.order_lines row. Location: the Model section SQL, JOIN raw_shop.order_lines with no GROUP BY of mart_daily_revenue's plan.

PASS only if the review names this defect and ties it to that part of the plan. Generic advice with no tie to this table or step does not count. FAIL otherwise.

2-dedupllm

Answer key, category dedup. Planted defect: the source delivers at least once (Pub/Sub export, retried when an ack times out) and the Load section uses a plain INSERT with no MERGE or dedup on order_id, so redelivered rows are duplicated. Location: the Load section, INSERT INTO ... SELECT of mart_daily_revenue's plan.

PASS only if the review names this defect and ties it to that part of the plan. Generic advice with no tie to this table or step does not count. FAIL otherwise.

3-scanllm

Answer key, category scan. Planted defect: raw_shop.orders (about 4 TB) is created without PARTITION BY or CLUSTER BY and the query filters on DATE(order_ts), so every run scans the whole table. Location: the Source section DDL and query of mart_daily_revenue's plan.

PASS only if the review names this defect and ties it to that part of the plan. Generic advice with no tie to this table or step does not count. FAIL otherwise.

Evidence (what the judge was shown; for an image, a description of what was sent)
The plan isn't ready to build. I appended a `## Data review (2026-11-04)` section to `plan.md` with the verdict **NOT READY**. I didn't touch the rest of the plan.

I didn't dry-run any queries. The cost figures come from the plan's "about 2.5 TB" and the 10 GB dev limit in `AGENTS.md`. I also only searched the repo for an existing `web_sessions` model, not the warehouse. The Google companion skills weren't confirmed installed, so the vendor second pass wasn't run.

**Blockers**
1. **Personal data exposed.** `visitor_email` and `ip_address` sit in a dataset readable by `allAuthenticatedUsers`, which means any Google account. The contract calls it `internal`. The model SQL doesn't select those columns, so Access and Model disagree.
2. **Double counting.** Beacons are resent on failure, so `page_views` has duplicates and `SUM(amount)` overstates. The source has no event id to dedupe on.
3. **Not rerunnable.** The load is a bare `INSERT`, so rerunning a `ds`, or the rollout's rebuild, appends duplicates. It needs a `MERGE` or a delete-then-insert for one `ds`.
4. **Wrong key.** Grain is session per `ds`, but the contract's key is `session_id` alone. A session that spans midnight gets two rows with the same key.
5. **Cost and partitioning.** No partitioning, clustering or `require_partition_filter` is specified. The source filters on `DATE(viewed_at)` and the model on `ds`. If the source isn't partitioned on `ds`, each run could scan about 2.5 TB, roughly 250 times the dev limit.

**Should-fix:**
- The consumer and decision aren't named.
- There is no ODCS contract file in `contracts/`.
- "Check row counts" is the only quality gate, and nothing backs the 07:00 freshness SLA.
- Late-arriving data isn't handled.
- Downstream consumers aren't listed.
- Rollback restores the whole table, which would discard any `ds` written after the snapshot. Step 1 also fails on a second run and sets no snapshot expiry.

I also added two nits: say it's a `ds` snapshot, and document the unit of `amount`.

The first decision for you is whether `visitor_email` and `ip_address` should exist in this table at all. That settles blocker 1 and shapes the contract and access design. Do you want me to draft the plan edits once you've decided, or grill you on the open questions first?

Evidence (what the judge was shown; for an image, a description of what was sent)
I reviewed the plan and appended a `## Data review (2026-11-04)` section to `plan.md`. **The verdict is NOT READY: there are four blockers.**

I couldn't run a `bq` dry run, because this session has no shell. The cost finding rests on the plan's own 2.5 TB figure, so run a dry run before you close that item.

**Blockers**
1. **Personal data is public.** `public_web_metrics` is readable by `allAuthenticatedUsers` (any Google account). It lists `visitor_email` and `ip_address`, while the contract says `classification: internal`.
2. **The load isn't idempotent.** It's a bare `INSERT`, so rerunning a `ds` or rebuilding a range appends duplicates.
3. **Resent beacons double-count.** The source is resent on network failure, `SUM(amount)` counts the duplicates, and the schema has no event id to dedupe on.
4. **Every run scans the full table.** Nothing is partitioned or clustered, and the source filter `DATE(viewed_at) = @ds` can't prune either. Each run bills about 2.5 TB, against a `dev_max_bytes_billed` of 10 GB.

**Should-fix**
- The contract key is `session_id`, but the grain is per session per `ds`. It should be `(ds, session_id)`.
- Late-arriving rows aren't handled, and `ds` has two competing definitions.
- There are no tests and no source freshness behind the 07:00 UTC SLA.
- The model SQL outputs 3 columns, but the Access section lists 5.
- Rollback is thin. The snapshot step fails on a rerun, and nothing says what the row counts are compared against.
- No contract file exists in `contracts/`.
- No consumer is named, and downstream models aren't listed.

I found no existing model or table that already does this job. I only edited the appended section, so the rest of the plan is as you wrote it.

Do you want to fix these in the plan yourself, or should I draft the edits? The cleanest order is the access/classification decision, then the dedupe key, since both change the contract.

Evidence (what the judge was shown; for an image, a description of what was sent)
The plan is not ready to build. I ran the data review and appended `## Data review (2026-11-04)` to `plan.md`. The verdict is `NOT READY`, with four blockers.

I didn't run a BigQuery dry run. The source table doesn't appear to exist in `example-project-dev`, so the cost figure uses the plan's own 2.5 TB. The repo has no existing models or contracts, so nothing already serves this consumer.

**Blockers**
1. **Personal data (item 7):** `public_web_metrics` is readable by `allAuthenticatedUsers` and lists `visitor_email` and `ip_address`. The contract says `classification: internal`. The model's SELECT doesn't produce either column anyway.
2. **Rerun safety (item 5):** the load is a bare `INSERT INTO`, so rerunning a `ds` duplicates every row. The rollout's "rebuild for the ds range" has no written mechanism, so it needs an idempotent `MERGE` or delete-and-insert.
3. **Duplicate beacons (item 8):** the source resends beacons on network failure, so `SUM(amount)` double-counts. The source has no event id to dedupe on.
4. **Cost (item 6):** the source DDL shows no `PARTITION BY`, and the target has no partitioning, clustering or `require_partition_filter`. If `ds` doesn't prune, each run scans about 2.5 TB. `dev_max_bytes_billed` is 10 GB, so a dev run would fail.

**Should-fix**
- **Late data:** the source query filters on `DATE(viewed_at) = @ds` and the model on `ds = @ds`. These can disagree, and there is no lookback window for resent beacons.
- **Primary key:** `primary_key: session_id` should be `(ds, session_id)`.
- **Missing sections:** the contract file isn't under `contracts/`, no consumer is named, and no downstream readers are listed.
- **Quality checks:** "check row counts" has no expected value, and there is no freshness check for 07:00 UTC.
- **Rollback:** the snapshot has no expiry, it can't exist on the first build, and it covers the whole table while the rebuild covers a range.

The one nit is that the plan never says it is a `ds` snapshot.

I didn't edit the rest of the plan; the skill leaves the fixes to the author. The two decisions I need from you are which columns, if any, should identify a visitor, and how to dedupe the beacons. I can draft the plan edits once you've settled those.

Evidence (what the judge was shown; for an image, a description of what was sent)
I reviewed the plan and appended a `## Data review (2026-11-04)` section to `plan.md`. **Verdict: NOT READY**, because there are six blockers. I didn't run a BigQuery dry-run, since the plan has no target DDL. The 2.5 TB scan figure comes from the plan itself and wasn't measured.

**Blockers**
1. **Public access to personal data:** `allAuthenticatedUsers` can read a dataset with `visitor_email` and `ip_address`, while the contract says `internal`. There is also no erasure path.
2. **Reruns duplicate rows:** the load is a bare `INSERT`, so rerunning a `ds` inserts every row again.
3. **Double-counted amounts:** the source resends beacons, so `page_views` has duplicates and `SUM(amount)` overcounts. The source has no event id to dedupe on.
4. **Column mismatch:** `visitor_email` and `ip_address` are in Access but not in the source schema or the model's SELECT.
5. **Wrong primary key:** `primary_key: session_id` contradicts the grain of one row per session per `ds`. The key should be `(ds, session_id)`.
6. **No partitioning:** neither table is partitioned or clustered, and neither has `require_partition_filter`. Each daily run would scan about 2.5 TB, against a dev limit of 10 GB. The source query's `DATE(viewed_at)` filter would also defeat pruning on a `ds` partition.

**Should-fix**
- No consumer or decision is named.
- The contract has no schema or quality rules.
- `ds` is defined two different ways in the plan.
- The row-count check has no rule.
- No downstream consumers are listed.
- The rollback fails on a rerun or a new table, and the restore statement isn't written.
- Late-arriving resent beacons have no handling.

I found no existing model or contract that already serves this need. I only searched the repo, not the warehouse. I didn't edit the rest of the plan, so you resolve the findings, and I'll re-review once you have.

6. **No partitioning:** neither table is partitioned or clustered, and neither has `require_partition_filter`. Each daily run would scan about 2.5 TB, against a dev limit of 10 GB. The source query's `DATE(viewed_at)` filter would also defeat pruning on a `ds` partition.

**Should-fix**
- No consumer or decision is named.
- The contract has no schema or quality rules.
- `ds` is defined two different ways in the plan.
- The row-count check has no rule.
- No downstream consumers are listed.
- The rollback fails on a rerun or a new table, and the restore statement isn't written.
- Late-arriving resent beacons have no handling.

I found no existing model or contract that already serves this need. I only searched the repo, not the warehouse. I didn't edit the rest of the plan, so you resolve the findings, and I'll re-review once you have.

Evidence (what the judge was shown; for an image, a description of what was sent)
I reviewed the plan and appended a `## Data review (2026-11-04)` section to it. **Verdict: NOT READY**, with four blockers.

**Blockers**
1. **Personal data is exposed.** `public_web_metrics` is readable by `allAuthenticatedUsers`, which is any Google account. It lists `visitor_email` and `ip_address`, but the contract says `classification: internal`. The model SQL doesn't select those columns, so the Access and Model sections also disagree.
2. **Amounts will be double-counted.** The source beacons are resent on network failure, so `page_views` has duplicates. `SUM(amount)` counts them twice, and there's no event id to dedupe on.
3. **The load isn't idempotent.** A bare `INSERT` appends the same rows again if a `ds` is rerun. It needs a `MERGE` or a delete-and-insert of the partition.
4. **The key doesn't match the grain.** The grain is one row per session per ds, but the contract's `primary_key` is `session_id` alone. A session that crosses midnight would produce two rows. It should be `[ds, session_id]`.

**Should-fix**
- **`ds` definition:** the source query uses `DATE(viewed_at) = @ds` and the model uses `ds = @ds`. The plan also doesn't say how late or resent beacons are handled.
- **Cost:** no partitioning or `require_partition_filter` is stated. If `ds` isn't the partition column, each run scans about 2.5 TB. That is roughly 250 times the 10 GB `dev_max_bytes_billed`.
- **Rollout:**
  - The snapshot step fails on a second run because the snapshot already exists.
  - The rebuild step is unspecified.
  - The row-count check has no expected number.
  - There's no restore command.
  - The snapshot also copies the personal-data columns.
- **Gaps:**
  - No consumer is named, and `amount` isn't defined.
  - No tests are listed, and the 07:00 UTC freshness SLA has no matching schedule.
  - No downstream readers are listed.
- **Nit:** the plan never says it's a ds snapshot rather than SCD2.

**What I didn't verify**
- The 2.5 TB figure comes from the plan, not a measurement. I ran no `bq` dry-runs because the plan names no dev table to run them against.
- Nothing else in the repo mentions sessions or page views, so I found no existing model that already answers this.
- I didn't change the rest of the plan. The author resolves the findings.

Run 1 66.7% 2 turns · $0.04 · 12:01:30 UTC
✓ pass 1-dedup

judge votes: PASS PASS FAIL

Judge votes✓ ✓ ✗
Evidence (what the judge was shown; for an image, a description of what was sent)
Review of the plan. The issues below are ordered by severity.

**Blocking**

1. **Duplicate beacons will inflate `amount`.** The source says beacons are "resent on network failure", so `raw_web.page_views` will contain duplicate rows. `SUM(amount)` will double-count them. The plan has no dedup step, and the table has no event or beacon ID to dedup on. You need one of these:
   - a unique beacon or event ID added upstream, then `QUALIFY ROW_NUMBER() OVER (PARTITION BY event_id ...) = 1` before aggregating; or
   - an agreed natural key, such as `(session_id, viewed_at, amount)`, with the caveat that it can drop legitimate identical events.

2. **The model selects columns the source doesn't have.** The Access section lists `visitor_email` and `ip_address`, but the source table has only `ds, viewed_at, session_id, amount`, and the model SQL emits only `ds, session_id, amount`. Either the schema is wrong or the plan is incomplete.

3. **PII is exposed to everyone.** If `visitor_email` and `ip_address` are included, the plan grants `allAuthenticatedUsers` read access to them. That is every signed-in Google account, not just your org. It also conflicts with the contract's `classification: internal`. Restrict readers to specific groups, drop or hash the PII columns, and set the classification to match what the table contains. The dataset name `public_web_metrics` suggests the exposure may be deliberate. Confirm that it isn't.

4. **The source filter and the model filter disagree, and the source query is a full scan.** The Source section filters on `DATE(viewed_at) = @ds`. The model and load filter on `ds = @ds`. If `ds` is not derived from `viewed_at` in UTC, the two will return different rows. Late-arriving beacons are a likely cause. Also, `DATE(viewed_at)` on a 2.5 TB table prevents partition pruning if the table is partitioned on `ds`. Pick one definition of the day, document it, and filter on the partition column.

**Should fix**

5. **The load isn't idempotent.** A plain `INSERT ... WHERE ds = @ds` will duplicate rows on any retry or rerun. The rollout step "rebuild for the ds range" has the same problem. Use `MERGE` on `(ds, session_id)`, or delete the partition and insert in one transaction, or use `INSERT OVERWRITE`/partition replacement.

6. **Grain and primary key conflict.** The model grain is one row per session per `ds`, but the contract declares `primary_key: session_id`. A session that spans midnight would produce two rows with the same key. Either make the key `(ds, session_id)` or define sessions so they can't cross days.

7. **Rollback is weaker than it looks.**
   - The snapshot is taken once, before the rebuild. It won't cover reruns or later changes.
   - "Time travel (7 days)" and the snapshot are both restore paths. State which one is primary, and note that restoring will undo any good data that landed after the snapshot.
   - The plan has no step to drop `_bak` after validation. Snapshot storage costs money and the table will go stale.

8. **The validation is too thin.** "Check row counts" would pass with the duplicate-inflation bug from item 1. Add these checks:
   - uniqueness on `(ds, session_id)`;
   - `SUM(amount)` compared with the source after dedup;
   - null checks on `session_id` and `amount`;
   - a comparison against the previous day's volume.

**Minor**

- `NUMERIC` sums can overflow on very large aggregates. That's unlikely here, but it's worth a note.
- `freshness_sla: 07:00 UTC` has no upstream dependency or monitoring described. Say what happens if the source is late.
- The plan has no tests or alerts, and no definition of "session" (timeout, cross-day behavior).
- The `ds` range for the rebuild is unspecified, and so is the cost estimate for scanning 2.5 TB per day if the filter doesn't prune.

**Recommendation:** don't build yet. Items 1–4 are correctness or security problems that would ship wrong numbers or leak data. Items 5–6 will bite on the first rerun. I can draft a revised plan with these fixed if you want, though items 2 and 3 need a decision from you on what the table should contain and who should read it.

Run 2 66.7% 2 turns · $0.04 · 12:02:26 UTC
✓ pass 1-dedup

judge votes: PASS PASS PASS

Judge votes✓ ✓ ✓
Evidence (what the judge was shown; for an image, a description of what was sent)
**Review of `plan.md`: not ready to build.** There are three blocking problems and several smaller ones.

**Blocking**

1. **Duplicate beacons inflate `amount`.** The source section says beacons are resent on network failure. `SUM(amount)` in the Model and Load sections will count every resend twice. `page_views` has no event or beacon ID to dedupe on. Either add one upstream, or define a dedupe key such as `(session_id, viewed_at, amount)` and say what its limits are. Add a uniqueness or reconciliation check as well.
2. **The load isn't idempotent.** `INSERT INTO fct_web_sessions` appends. Any retry or backfill duplicates rows, and the Rollout step "Rebuild for the ds range" does the same unless the range is cleared first. Use `MERGE` on the key, or a partition overwrite (delete the `ds`, then insert) in one transaction.
3. **PII is exposed to everyone.** The dataset `public_web_metrics` is readable by `allAuthenticatedUsers` and lists `visitor_email` and `ip_address`. The contract says the classification is `internal`. Those two things conflict. Restrict the readers to a named group, or drop or hash the PII columns. Neither column is produced by the Model query, so the column list and the model also disagree.

**Should fix**

4. **The primary key is wrong.** The contract says `primary_key: session_id`, but the grain is one row per session per `ds`. A session that crosses midnight appears under two `ds` values. The key should be `(ds, session_id)`.
5. **`ds` is defined two ways.** The Source example filters on `DATE(viewed_at) = @ds`. The Model filters on `ds = @ds`. Pick one and document what `ds` means: event date or load date. If `ds` is the partition column, the `DATE(viewed_at)` form may skip partition pruning and scan the full ~2.5 TB table.
6. **Late data isn't handled.** Resent beacons arrive late, but the plan has a 07:00 UTC freshness SLA and no lookback window for reprocessing recent `ds` values. State how late a beacon can arrive and how many days you will re-merge.
7. **The rollout checks are weak.** Row counts won't catch duplicated `amount`, because the session count stays the same even when the sums are wrong. Add a check that compares total `amount` and distinct sessions against the source. Also:
   - Say whether the snapshot covers the whole table or only the range, and give it an expiry.
   - Spell out the restore command. "Restore from bak or time travel" is vague.
   - Note that time travel's 7-day window limits how long the undo stays available.

I can draft a revised plan with a deduping `MERGE` load and tighter access and contract sections. Say if you want that.

5. **`ds` is defined two ways.** The Source example filters on `DATE(viewed_at) = @ds`. The Model filters on `ds = @ds`. Pick one and document what `ds` means: event date or load date. If `ds` is the partition column, the `DATE(viewed_at)` form may skip partition pruning and scan the full ~2.5 TB table.
6. **Late data isn't handled.** Resent beacons arrive late, but the plan has a 07:00 UTC freshness SLA and no lookback window for reprocessing recent `ds` values. State how late a beacon can arrive and how many days you will re-merge.
7. **The rollout checks are weak.** Row counts won't catch duplicated `amount`, because the session count stays the same even when the sums are wrong. Add a check that compares total `amount` and distinct sessions against the source. Also:
   - Say whether the snapshot covers the whole table or only the range, and give it an expiry.
   - Spell out the restore command. "Restore from bak or time travel" is vague.
   - Note that time travel's 7-day window limits how long the undo stays available.

I can draft a revised plan with a deduping `MERGE` load and tighter access and contract sections. Say if you want that.

✗ fail 1-dedup

judge votes: FAIL FAIL FAIL

Judge votes✗ ✗ ✗
Evidence (what the judge was shown; for an image, a description of what was sent)
The plan has two blocking problems, the PII exposure and the double counting of resent beacons. Several smaller issues follow them. I haven't run anything against the plan.

## Blocking

1. **PII is exposed to every authenticated user.**
   - *Where:* Access section.
   - *Problem:* `public_web_metrics` is readable by `allAuthenticatedUsers` and lists `visitor_email` and `ip_address`. That group means any Google account, not just your org. It also contradicts `classification: internal`.
   - *Mismatch:* The model SQL never selects those two columns, so the Access section doesn't match the model.
   - *Fix:* Drop the two columns, or restrict the dataset to specific groups and set the classification to match. Settle this before anything is built.

2. **Resent beacons will inflate `amount`.**
   - *Where:* Source says beacons are "resent on network failure," so `raw_web.page_views` will contain duplicates.
   - *Problem:* `SUM(amount)` double-counts them, and the table has no event ID to dedup on.
   - *Fix:* Add a dedup key such as an event or beacon ID, or define one from `(session_id, viewed_at, …)`. Dedup before aggregating, and say which duplicates count as the same event.

3. **The load isn't idempotent.**
   - *Where:* Load uses a plain `INSERT … WHERE ds = @ds`, and Rollout step 2 says "rebuild for the ds range."
   - *Problem:* Any rerun or backfill appends a second copy of each day.
   - *Fix:* Use `MERGE`, or delete the partition and then insert (or overwrite the partition) in one step.

## Should fix

4. **The primary key contradicts the grain.** The grain is one row per session per ds, but the contract says `primary_key: session_id`. A session that crosses midnight appears under two `ds` values. The key should be `(ds, session_id)`, or the plan should define how cross-day sessions are handled.

5. **The `ds` definition is inconsistent.**
   - *Mismatch:* The source query filters `DATE(viewed_at) = @ds`, and the model filters `ds = @ds`. These can differ with timezones or late arrivals.
   - *Fix:* Pick one definition.
   - *Cost:* If the table is partitioned on `ds`, filtering on `DATE(viewed_at)` can scan the full 2.5 TB. The source example also uses `SELECT *`. Confirm the partitioning and select only the columns you need.

6. **Late data isn't handled, and the SLA is undefined.** Resent beacons arrive late. The 07:00 UTC freshness SLA needs a stated lateness window, and a plan for re-merging recent days when late events land.

7. **The rollout checks are weak.**
   - *Check:* Row counts won't catch double-counted amounts. Compare `SUM(amount)` and distinct sessions per `ds` against the old table or an independent query.
   - *Snapshot:* `CREATE SNAPSHOT TABLE … _bak` fails if the snapshot already exists. Use a dated name.
   - *Rollback:* Time travel covers 7 days, so say when the snapshot gets dropped.

## Minor

- `amount` is nullable, so `SUM` can return NULL for a session. Decide whether to use `COALESCE`.
- The plan has no tests, such as unique `(ds, session_id)`, not-null, and accepted ranges. These would fit the contract section.

I'd fix items 1–3 and settle the key and `ds` definitions (4 and 5) before building.

Run 4 66.7% 2 turns · $0.04 · 12:04:03 UTC
✓ pass 1-dedup

judge votes: PASS PASS FAIL

Judge votes✓ ✓ ✗
Evidence (what the judge was shown; for an image, a description of what was sent)
I found several problems in the plan. The first five would produce wrong data or expose it, so I'd fix them before building.

**Blocking**

1. **Duplicate beacons inflate `amount`.** The source says beacons are resent on network failure, but the model runs `SUM(amount)` on raw rows. Every resend is double-counted. `page_views` also has no event or beacon ID to dedupe on. We need either an ID added upstream or a dedupe key such as `(session_id, viewed_at, amount)`, applied before aggregating.

2. **The load isn't idempotent.** Step 2 of the rollout, "Rebuild for the ds range", uses a plain `INSERT INTO`. Rebuilding or retrying a day appends a second copy of it. Use `MERGE`, or delete and insert by `ds` in one transaction. A partition overwrite also works.

3. **PII is exposed to a broad audience.** The `public_web_metrics` dataset is readable by `allAuthenticatedUsers`, and the column list includes `visitor_email` and `ip_address`. That means any signed-in Google account could read them. It also contradicts `classification: internal`. Restrict the readers to specific groups and drop or hash the PII columns. Also, the model SQL doesn't produce those two columns, so the access list and the model disagree.

4. **The primary key contradicts the grain.** The grain is one row per session per `ds`, but the contract says `primary_key: session_id`. A session that crosses midnight appears under two `ds` values. The key should be `(ds, session_id)`.

5. **The `ds` filters disagree.** The source query filters on `DATE(viewed_at) = @ds` and the model filters on `ds = @ds`. These differ for late-arriving or resent beacons, and for any timezone mismatch. Pick one definition and document it. Also confirm the table is partitioned on `ds`. If it isn't, or if you filter on `DATE(viewed_at)`, each run scans the full 2.5 TB.

**Should fix**

6. **Rollback is weaker than it looks.** Restoring from the snapshot overwrites any `ds` loaded after it was taken. Scope the restore to the affected `ds` range. Also set an expiry on `fct_web_sessions_bak`, and make step 1 fail or use a dated name if the snapshot already exists.
7. **The validation is too thin.** Row counts alone won't catch the double-counting in item 1. Add checks that `(ds, session_id)` is unique, that `SUM(amount)` reconciles against deduplicated source, and that there are no null `session_id` values.
8. **The 07:00 UTC freshness SLA ignores late beacons.** Say how long late data is accepted, and whether previous days get reprocessed.
9. **Check the type of `amount`.** `NUMERIC` on a page-view table is unusual. If it's a revenue or value field, state that. If it's a count, a different type may fit better.

I can rewrite the plan with these changes if you'd like.

✓ pass 1-dedup

judge votes: PASS FAIL PASS

Judge votes✓ ✗ ✓
Evidence (what the judge was shown; for an image, a description of what was sent)
I read the plan. I'd fix the first five issues below before building.

**Blockers**

1. **Duplicate beacons inflate `amount`.** The source says beacons are resent on network failure, so the same page view can land more than once. `SUM(amount)` then double counts. The schema has no event ID to dedupe on. Either add one (for example `event_id` or a beacon hash) and dedupe before aggregating, or state the dedupe key.
2. **The load isn't idempotent.** `INSERT INTO ... WHERE ds = @ds` duplicates the whole partition on any rerun or backfill. Rollout step 2 ("rebuild for the ds range") would hit this directly. Use `MERGE`, or delete the partition and then insert it, or overwrite the partition.
3. **The access section exposes PII.** `public_web_metrics` is readable by `allAuthenticatedUsers`, which means anyone with a Google account. The listed columns include `visitor_email` and `ip_address`, and the contract says `classification: internal`. Pick one of these:
   - Drop those columns.
   - Move the table to a restricted dataset.
   - Hash or mask the columns and limit the readers.

   The model SQL doesn't select those columns, and the source table doesn't have them, so the column list also contradicts the rest of the plan.
4. **The primary key contradicts the grain.** The grain is one row per session per `ds`, but the contract says `primary_key: session_id`. A session that crosses midnight appears under two `ds` values. The key should be `(ds, session_id)`. The plan should also say which `ds` a cross-midnight session belongs to.
5. **`ds` and `viewed_at` are filtered inconsistently.** The source example uses `DATE(viewed_at) = @ds`, but the model uses `ds = @ds`. These can differ because of timezone or late arrival. The first form also wraps the column in a function, which can defeat partition pruning on a 2.5 TB table. Define how `ds` is derived, and use only the partition column in the filter.

**Should fix**

6. **Late data against the SLA.** Resent beacons arrive late, but the plan has no lateness window or reprocessing of recent partitions. A 07:00 UTC freshness SLA conflicts with that. Decide whether to reprocess the last N days.
7. **The rollout checks are weak.**
   - "Check row counts" has no expected value. Compare against `COUNT(DISTINCT session_id)` from the source for the same `ds`, and add a check that the key is unique.
   - The snapshot step fails on a rerun because `_bak` already exists, and it fails on first build because the table doesn't exist yet.
   - The snapshot has no expiry.
   - The restore command isn't written out.
8. **Smaller gaps.** The partitioning and clustering of the target aren't specified. `SUM(amount)` returns NULL if all amounts are null, so it may need a `COALESCE`. The "Model" and "Load" sections duplicate the same SQL, so they can drift apart.

If you want, I can edit the plan to fix these. For items 1, 3 and 4 I need decisions from you: the dedupe key, which columns should be exposed, and how to assign cross-midnight sessions.

The plan for the web sessions model is at docs/wraith/2026-10-12-web-sessions/plan.md. Review it before we build it.

Graders — what "good" means for this case
1-dedupllm

Answer key, category dedup. Planted defect: the source delivers at least once (tracker beacons, resent on network failure) and the Load section uses a plain INSERT with no MERGE or dedup on session_id, so redelivered rows are duplicated. Location: the Load section, INSERT INTO ... SELECT of fct_web_sessions's plan.

PASS only if the review names this defect and ties it to that part of the plan. Generic advice with no tie to this table or step does not count. FAIL otherwise.

2-scanllm

Answer key, category scan. Planted defect: raw_web.page_views (about 2.5 TB) is created without PARTITION BY or CLUSTER BY and the query filters on DATE(viewed_at), so every run scans the whole table. Location: the Source section DDL and query of fct_web_sessions's plan.

PASS only if the review names this defect and ties it to that part of the plan. Generic advice with no tie to this table or step does not count. FAIL otherwise.

3-piillm

Answer key, category pii. Planted defect: columns visitor_email, ip_address are personal data and the dataset public_web_metrics is readable by allAuthenticatedUsers. Location: the Access section of fct_web_sessions's plan.

PASS only if the review names this defect and ties it to that part of the plan. Generic advice with no tie to this table or step does not count. FAIL otherwise.

judge votes: PASS PASS PASS

Judge votes✓ ✓ ✓
Evidence (what the judge was shown; for an image, a description of what was sent)
I reviewed the plan and appended a `## Data review (2026-11-04)` section to `plan.md`. The verdict is **NOT READY**, with four blockers.

**Blockers**
1. **Public personal data.** `open_support_data` is readable by `allUsers` and exposes `requester_name`, `requester_email` and `requester_phone`. The contract calls the data `internal`. Remove the public grant, and either drop those columns or classify and protect them.
2. **Double-counting.** The webhook is replayed on failure, so the source will contain duplicate events. `SUM(amount)` would count them twice, and the source has no event id to dedupe on.
3. **Unsafe load and rollout.** Rollout step 1 is `CREATE OR REPLACE TABLE`, which overwrites the table and has no rollback. The MERGE also never removes tickets that disappear when you rerun a ds.
4. **No partitioning.** Neither table has partitioning, clustering or `require_partition_filter`. Each run would scan 1.8 TB, which is 180 times the 10 GB dev byte cap. That figure is arithmetic from the plan's size, not a dry run.

**Should-fix**
- **Key and contract:** The contract key is `ticket_id`, but the grain is per ticket per ds. There is also no ODCS contract file in `contracts/`.
- **Definition of ds:** The source query filters on `DATE(event_ts)` and the model filters on `ds`, and these can disagree. The access column list also doesn't match the model SQL.
- **Missing sections:** There is no late-arrival handling, no tests, no named consumer and no downstream list.

I did not run a dry run or look for an existing equivalent table in the warehouse. The check for an existing model covered only this repo, where nothing else mentions these tables.

The `allUsers` grant is an IAM change, so I'd also run `wraith:security-audit` on it. I left the rest of the plan untouched for the author to resolve.

Evidence (what the judge was shown; for an image, a description of what was sent)
I reviewed the plan and appended a `## Data review (2026-11-04)` section to `plan.md`. The verdict is **NOT READY**, with six blockers.

I didn't run any dry-runs or `dbt ls`, because the repo has no dev table, `dbt/` project or `contracts/` content yet. The cost figures come from the plan's own 1.8 TB, not from a measurement. I found no existing model or contract that already covers this, so there is no premise blocker.

**Blockers**
1. **Public access to personal data.** `Readers: allUsers` makes the dataset public, and it exposes requester name, email and phone.
2. **Wrong classification.** The contract says `classification: internal`, but the table holds personal data. It needs reclassifying, policy tags on the three columns, and an erasure path.
3. **Missing columns.** The `requester_*` columns aren't in the source DDL or the model SQL. The plan doesn't say where they come from.
4. **Double-counting.** The webhook is replayed on failure and the source has no event id, so `SUM(amount)` double-counts replayed events.
5. **Full-table scans.** No partitioning or `require_partition_filter` is stated, so each run scans the full 1.8 TB. The dev cap is 10 GB.
6. **No safe rollout.** Rollout step 1 is `CREATE OR REPLACE TABLE`, and the plan has no rollback or backfill path.

**Should-fix items** (full detail in the file)
- `primary_key: ticket_id` contradicts the one-row-per-ticket-per-ds grain. It should be `[ds, ticket_id]`.
- The source filters on `DATE(event_ts)` but the model filters on `ds`, so the two can disagree.
- The MERGE leaves stale rows when a `ds` is rerun.
- The plan has no tests or freshness check, and no named consumer.
- The plan lists no downstream models.
- Late-arriving events aren't handled.

The `allUsers` grant is an IAM change, so run `wraith:security-audit` on it. I didn't edit the rest of the plan, so the author needs to resolve the findings. I can draft the fixes if you'd like.

Evidence (what the judge was shown; for an image, a description of what was sent)
I reviewed the plan and appended a `## Data review (2026-11-04)` section to it. The verdict is **NOT READY**, with five blockers.

I didn't run a dry run, so the cost finding rests on the plan's "about 1.8 TB" figure and the query text. The plan's tables aren't in `example-project-dev`, and the `dbt/` and `contracts/` directories are empty.

**Blockers**
1. **Public PII.** Readers are `allUsers` on a dataset with requester name, email and phone, while the contract says `classification: internal`. Remove `allUsers`, reclassify and tag the PII columns, or drop them.
2. **Columns without a source.** The `requester_*` columns aren't in `raw_support.ticket_events` and the model SQL doesn't produce them.
3. **Replays and late data.** The webhook is "replayed on failure" and there's no event id, so `SUM(amount)` double counts replayed events. Late-arriving events aren't handled either.
4. **Destructive rollout.** `CREATE OR REPLACE TABLE` has no rollback and no backfill path.
5. **Cost.** The source is about 1.8 TB. The sample query filters on `DATE(event_ts)`, which doesn't prune the `ds` partition. No partitioning or `require_partition_filter` is stated. The 10 GB dev cap would fail a full scan.

**Should-fix items**
- The contract's primary key is `ticket_id`, but the grain is per `ds`, so the key should be `(ds, ticket_id)`.
- The MERGE scans the whole target and never removes tickets that disappear from the source.
- The plan names no consumer, has no tests or schema, and lists no downstream readers.
- The `dim_` name doesn't fit a daily snapshot.

The review also has one nit, about stating a currency for `amount`.

I did not run the Google vendor second pass (`datalineage-bigquery-asset-impact-analysis`, `bigquery-optimization`). Both set an attribution tag that reports usage to Google. Do you want it run? Separately, tell me whether the `requester_*` columns belong in this table. That answer decides two of the blockers.

Evidence (what the judge was shown; for an image, a description of what was sent)
I reviewed the plan and appended a `## Data review (2026-11-04)` section to `plan.md`. The verdict is **NOT READY**, with six blockers.

Step 0 found nothing to reuse. A repo search for `ticket_summary` and `ticket_events` matched only the plan. I didn't query the warehouse, and I didn't run any dry-runs. The source table isn't in the repo, so the cost concern below is reasoned, not measured.

**Blockers**
1. **Public access:** `Readers: allUsers` makes the table readable by anyone on the internet. Replace it with named groups.
2. **Personal data:** the model exposes requester name, email and phone, but the contract says `classification: internal`. There is no classification, policy tags or erasure path for them.
3. **Columns and key:** the requester columns aren't in the source schema or the model SQL, so the model can't produce them. The contract's `primary_key: ticket_id` also contradicts the grain, which needs `(ds, ticket_id)`.
4. **Duplicate events:** the webhook replays on failure, and the source has no event id. `SUM(amount)` would double-count replayed events.
5. **Rollout:** `CREATE OR REPLACE TABLE` drops existing data. There is no rollback and no pass or fail threshold for "check row counts".
6. **Cost:** the source is about 1.8 TB. The plan doesn't say it's partitioned on `ds`, doesn't require a partition filter, and has no cost estimate. The review asks for one measured against the 10 GB dev limit.

**Should fix**
- The source query filters `DATE(event_ts) = @ds` while the model filters `ds = @ds`, and nothing says how late events are handled.
- The MERGE leaves stale rows when a ticket drops out of a `ds`, and there is no backfill path.
- The plan names no consumer, has no contract file in `contracts/`, has no tests, and lists no downstream models.

**Nits:** the `dim_` prefix for a per-day aggregate, and `amount` has no currency.

I didn't edit the rest of the plan; the author resolves the findings. Do you want me to draft fixes for the blockers, or run `wraith:grilling` on the plan next?

1. **Public personal data.** `Readers: allUsers` exposes requester name, email and phone to the internet. The contract classifies the dataset as `internal`.

- No partitioning, clustering or `require_partition_filter`. If the source is unpartitioned, each run could scan up to 1.8 TB, against a 10 GB dev cap.
- The MERGE leaves stale rows on rerun, and no backfill path is written.
- The contract has no versioning or quality rules, and the plan has no tests.
- No consumer, decision or downstream models are named.

Judge votes✓ ✓ ✓
Evidence (what the judge was shown; for an image, a description of what was sent)
**Verdict: NOT READY.** I appended a `## Data review (2026-11-04)` section to `plan.md`. I changed nothing else in the plan.

**Blockers** (resolve these before building):
1. **Public personal data.** `Readers: allUsers` exposes requester name, email and phone to the internet. The contract classifies the dataset as `internal`.
2. **Replays double-count.** The webhook is replayed on failure, but the model runs `SUM(amount)` with no event key or dedup. The plan also doesn't say how late-arriving events are handled.
3. **`ds` defined two ways.** The source query filters `DATE(event_ts) = @ds`, while the model filters `ds = @ds`. The plan doesn't say which one is the real `ds`.
4. **Unsafe rollout.** `CREATE OR REPLACE TABLE` has no rollback. "Check row counts" has no expected number.
5. **Key and columns don't match the grain.** The grain is one row per ticket per ds, but the contract's primary key is `ticket_id` alone. The contract and access lists include `requester_*` columns that the model SQL never produces.

**Should-fix:**
- No partitioning, clustering or `require_partition_filter`. If the source is unpartitioned, each run could scan up to 1.8 TB, against a 10 GB dev cap.
- The MERGE leaves stale rows on rerun, and no backfill path is written.
- The contract has no versioning or quality rules, and the plan has no tests.
- No consumer, decision or downstream models are named.

**Not done:**
- I ran no dry-run, because the tables aren't defined in the repo and I didn't query dev. The 1.8 TB figure is the plan's own, with no stated source.
- I didn't check whether the vendor second-pass skills are installed. They would set an attribution tag that reports usage to Google, so I'd ask you first.
- Step 0 only searched this repo. A ticket model could still exist elsewhere in the warehouse.

Do you want me to draft the plan revisions for the blockers? Alternatively, I could run `wraith:grilling` on the open design questions: which `ds` definition, the dedup key, and who the readers are.

✗ fail 1-scan

judge votes: FAIL FAIL FAIL

Judge votes✗ ✗ ✗
Evidence (what the judge was shown; for an image, a description of what was sent)
I read the plan. It isn't ready to build: there is one blocker, and several correctness problems behind it.

**Blocker**
- **Access (lines 40-43):** `allUsers` readers makes the dataset public to anyone on the internet. The column list also includes `requester_name`, `requester_email` and `requester_phone`, which is PII. That contradicts `classification: internal` (line 54). The model SQL only produces `ds, ticket_id, amount`, so the PII columns don't come from anywhere in the plan. Drop them from the column list and restrict readers to a specific group.

**Correctness**
- **Replays double-count:** The webhook is "replayed on failure" (line 11), and the table has no event ID. `SUM(amount)` will inflate totals whenever an event is delivered twice. The plan needs a dedup key, such as an event ID, or a different aggregation.
- **`ds` and `event_ts` disagree:** The source example filters on `DATE(event_ts) = @ds` (line 16). The model and MERGE filter on `ds = @ds`. If `ds` is load date and `event_ts` is event time, replays and late events land in different days depending on which one you use. Pick one and define it.
- **Primary key doesn't match the grain:** The grain is one row per ticket per `ds` (line 21), and the MERGE joins on `(ds, ticket_id)`. The contract says `primary_key: ticket_id` (line 53). It should be `(ds, ticket_id)`.
- **Rollout contradicts the Load step:** `CREATE OR REPLACE TABLE` (line 47) wipes the table. That makes sense once, but the plan doesn't say how history gets backfilled, or whether this step is separate from the daily MERGE. "Check row counts" has no pass/fail criterion. Compare against a source distinct-ticket count per `ds`, and check for null or duplicate keys.

**Cost and operations**
- **Partitioning:** The source is about 1.8 TB. `WHERE ds = @ds` only limits the scan if the table is partitioned on `ds`. The plan doesn't say it is, and it doesn't say how `dim_ticket_summary` is partitioned or clustered. The MERGE would scan the whole target too. State the partitioning and require the partition filter.
- **SLA without a schedule:** `freshness_sla: 07:00 UTC` has no schedule, no late-data window and no alerting. Say when the job runs and what happens when a replay arrives after the run.
- **Nulls and meaning of `amount`:** `amount` is a `NUMERIC` that can be null, and the plan doesn't explain what it means for a support ticket. Define it, and define how nulls are handled.

I'd fix the access section and the dedup question before anything else. The rest are quick edits once you've settled the `ds` definition. Do you want me to draft a revised plan?
