Run 2 33.3% 2 turns · $0.04 · 12:11:32 UTC
✗ fail 1-scan

judge votes: FAIL FAIL FAIL

Judge votes✗ ✗ ✗
Evidence (what the judge was shown; for an image, a description of what was sent)
The plan isn't ready to build. Items 1 to 3 are the ones to fix first.

**Blocking**

1. **Public access to PII.** `open_support_data` with readers `allUsers` makes the table world-readable. It also lists `requester_name`, `requester_email` and `requester_phone`, and the contract says `classification: internal`. The model query doesn't select those columns, so the Access section and the Model section disagree. Restrict readers to a named group and drop or mask the PII columns unless there's a documented need for them.
2. **Replays will double-count.** The webhook is "replayed on failure", and `ticket_events` has no event ID. `SUM(amount)` will inflate whenever an event is delivered twice. Add a unique event ID and dedupe before aggregating. If the source can't provide one, say how you'll guarantee idempotency.
3. **The rollout contradicts the load.** Step 1 is `CREATE OR REPLACE TABLE ... AS SELECT`, but the Load section is an incremental per-`ds` MERGE.
   - Replacing the table wipes history on every run and scans all 1.8 TB.
   - Pick one approach: a one-time backfill by partition, then the daily MERGE.
   - "Check row counts" is too weak. Add checks for uniqueness on `(ds, ticket_id)`, nulls, and a reconciliation of `SUM(amount)` against the source.

**Should fix**

4. **Date filters are inconsistent.** The source example filters on `DATE(event_ts) = @ds`, but the model and MERGE filter on `ds = @ds`. These can differ for late or replayed events. Define which one is authoritative. If `ds` is the partition column, filtering on `DATE(event_ts)` won't prune partitions on a 1.8 TB table.
5. **The primary key doesn't match the grain.** The grain is one row per ticket per `ds`, but the contract says `primary_key: ticket_id`. It should be `(ds, ticket_id)`.
6. **The MERGE reads the destination unpruned.** Add a `d.ds = @ds` predicate to limit the destination scan. It also never removes rows that disappear from the source. Say whether that's intended.
7. **Nothing supports the 07:00 UTC freshness SLA.** There's no schedule, upstream dependency, or late-arrival window. Specify the cutoff for late events and what happens when a replay lands after the load.

**Minor**

8. `amount` has no currency or unit, and its meaning isn't defined. For a support ticket that's unclear: is it a refund, a charge, or a cost?
9. The columns are described in two places (the Model and Access sections) with different lists. Keep a single schema definition.

I can revise the plan with these changes if you'd like. Two decisions are yours: whether the PII columns are needed, and how to dedupe events.

Run 3 66.7% 2 turns · $0.04 · 12:12:17 UTC
✗ fail 1-scan

judge votes: FAIL FAIL FAIL

Judge votes✗ ✗ ✗
Evidence (what the judge was shown; for an image, a description of what was sent)
The plan isn't ready to build. Two problems are blocking: it would make PII public, and it would double-count amounts.

**Blocking**

1. **Public PII (Access, lines 42-43).** `Readers: allUsers` makes the dataset public. The column list includes `requester_name`, `requester_email` and `requester_phone`. The contract also says `classification: internal`, which contradicts public access. Restrict readers to a specific group. Drop the requester columns or mask them, unless there's a documented need for them.
2. **Replayed webhooks double-count (lines 11, 24).** The source says the webhook is "replayed on failure", so `ticket_events` will contain duplicate events. `SUM(amount)` will then overstate totals. The table has no `event_id`, so there's nothing to dedupe on. Add a unique event ID, or dedupe on a defined key before aggregating.
3. **Rollout is destructive and unvalidated (lines 47-48).** `CREATE OR REPLACE TABLE` overwrites any existing table. "Check row counts" has no expected value to compare against. Build into a staging table, compare against a stated expectation (for example, distinct `ticket_id` per `ds` in the source), then swap. Say how history is backfilled.

**Correctness**

4. **`ds` and `event_ts` disagree (lines 16 vs 26).** The source query filters on `DATE(event_ts) = @ds`, but the model and MERGE filter on `ds = @ds`. Those can differ for late or replayed events and for timezone edges. Pick one definition. Also, wrapping `event_ts` in `DATE()` can defeat partition pruning.
5. **Primary key contradicts the grain (line 53 vs 21).** The grain is one row per ticket per `ds`, but the contract says `primary_key: ticket_id`. It should be `(ds, ticket_id)`. The MERGE already joins on both.
6. **Access columns don't match the model.** The model outputs `ds`, `ticket_id` and `amount`. The requester columns aren't in the source DDL or the model, so their origin is undefined.

**Gaps**

- The plan doesn't say whether `ticket_events` is partitioned on `ds`. At about 1.8 TB, an unpruned scan is costly, so state the partitioning and clustering.
- `SELECT *` on line 16 should list the columns.
- `amount` on a support ticket isn't defined: is it a refund, a charge, or something else? A `SUM` only makes sense once that's stated, and negative or reversal events need a rule.
- The 07:00 UTC freshness SLA has no schedule, no late-arrival window and no failure alerting.
- The `dim_` prefix is odd for a daily-grain table that is really a fact or snapshot. This is minor.

The MERGE itself is idempotent per `ds`, which is good. Once items 1-5 are fixed, I can help update the plan.

Evidence (what the judge was shown; for an image, a description of what was sent)
The plan isn't ready to build. Two items are blockers, and the rest should be fixed in the plan first.

**Blockers**

1. **Public access to PII.** The Access section gives `allUsers` read access to `open_support_data`, and lists `requester_name`, `requester_email` and `requester_phone`. That would make customer contact details world-readable. It also contradicts `classification: internal`. Restrict readers to a named group, and drop the PII columns or move them to a restricted table or policy-tagged columns.
2. **Replays double-count amounts.** The source is a webhook that is "replayed on failure", so duplicate events are expected. The model uses `SUM(amount)` and the schema has no event ID to dedupe on. Add a unique event ID, or a dedupe step such as `QUALIFY ROW_NUMBER() OVER (PARTITION BY event_id ...) = 1`, before aggregating.

**Correctness**

3. **The grain and key disagree.** The grain is one row per ticket per `ds`, but the contract says `primary_key: ticket_id`. It should be `(ds, ticket_id)`.
4. **The source and model filter differently.** The source query uses `DATE(event_ts) = @ds` and the model uses `ds = @ds`. Late or replayed events can have a `ds` that doesn't match their `event_ts` date, so the two filters can return different rows. Pick one definition and say how late data is handled. The MERGE has no handling for rows that disappear, so a replay or correction can leave stale rows.
5. **The table schema and the model don't match.** The Access section lists six columns, but the model produces three (`ds`, `ticket_id`, `amount`). `INSERT ROW` would fail or leave the PII columns null. Define the DDL explicitly.
6. **`amount` isn't defined.** The plan doesn't say what a ticket "amount" is, for example whether refunds count or whether the currency is always the same. Without that, the sum can't be validated.

**Cost and operations**

7. **The source scan is expensive.** The source is about 1.8 TB. `SELECT *` combined with `DATE(event_ts)` can defeat partition pruning. Filter on the partition column and select only the columns you need. Also say how the MERGE target is partitioned and clustered.
8. **The rollout is risky.** `CREATE OR REPLACE TABLE` drops the table's existing grants, schema and history. The plan has no backfill range, no staging or swap step, and no rollback. A row-count check alone won't catch the double-counting or the filter mismatch. Compare per-`ds` totals against the deduped source and check that the key is unique.
9. **The freshness SLA has no schedule.** The 07:00 UTC SLA has no schedule, no upstream dependency and no alerting behind it. The plan also lacks a data-quality contract: not-null and uniqueness tests, and a plan for late events.

Do you want me to revise the plan with these changes?

✗ fail 1-scan

judge votes: FAIL FAIL PASS

Judge votes✗ ✗ ✓
Evidence (what the judge was shown; for an image, a description of what was sent)
**Verdict: don't build this yet.** The access section is a blocker, and the source and keys have correctness problems.

**Blockers**
1. **Public PII exposure (Access, lines 42-43).**
   - `allUsers` makes the dataset readable by anyone on the internet.
   - The column list includes `requester_name`, `requester_email` and `requester_phone`.
   - The contract says `classification: internal`, so the two sections contradict each other.
   - Grant access to a named group instead. Either drop the PII columns or put them in a restricted table or policy-tagged columns.
2. **Columns don't match (lines 14, 24, 43).**
   - The source table has only `ds, event_ts, ticket_id, amount`.
   - The model emits only `ds, ticket_id, amount`.
   - The requester columns come from nowhere. You need a join to a ticket or requester source, or they should come out of the Access list.
3. **Replays will double-count (line 11).**
   - The webhook is "replayed on failure", so `ticket_events` can hold duplicate events.
   - `SUM(amount)` will then inflate totals.
   - There is no event ID to dedupe on. Either add one upstream or define a dedupe key, such as `ticket_id, event_ts, amount`, and say what it risks.

**Correctness**
4. **Two different date filters (lines 16 and 26).**
   - The source example filters on `DATE(event_ts) = @ds`. The model filters on `ds = @ds`.
   - If `ds` is the ingestion date, replays and late events land in different days depending on which filter runs.
   - Pick one and document what `ds` means.
5. **Primary key contradicts grain (lines 21 and 53).**
   - The grain is one row per ticket per ds, but the contract says `primary_key: ticket_id`.
   - It should be `(ds, ticket_id)`.
6. **Rollout conflicts with Load (line 47).**
   - `CREATE OR REPLACE TABLE ... AS SELECT` wipes the table, and it isn't the incremental MERGE path.
   - If it's a one-time creation, say so. Use `CREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS` with explicit partitioning, and define how backfill works.
   - "Check row counts" is too weak. Add checks for uniqueness on `(ds, ticket_id)`, non-null keys, and a sum reconciliation against the source. Also add a rollback step.

**Cost and ops**
7. **1.8 TB source with no partitioning plan.**
   - The doc doesn't say whether `ds` is a partition column. `DATE(event_ts)` on the source example won't prune if the table is partitioned on `ds`.
   - Confirm partitioning and clustering on both tables. Avoid `SELECT *`.
8. **No schedule or dependency for the 07:00 UTC SLA.**
   - Late replays can land after the run.
   - Add a trigger, a lookback window for reprocessing recent `ds` values, and alerting.
9. **Minor:** the aggregation SQL is duplicated in Model and Load, so define it once. The MERGE never deletes rows. That is fine if intended, but say so. The author is a team ("data team") and the status is draft, so there's no named approver.

I'd fix items 1-6 before building. Tell me which way you want items 3 and 4 to go and I can revise the plan.

Graders — what "good" means for this case
1-scanllm

Answer key, category scan. Planted defect: raw_support.ticket_events (about 1.8 TB) is created without PARTITION BY or CLUSTER BY and the query filters on DATE(event_ts), so every run scans the whole table. Location: the Source section DDL and query of dim_ticket_summary's plan.

PASS only if the review names this defect and ties it to that part of the plan. Generic advice with no tie to this table or step does not count. FAIL otherwise.

2-piillm

Answer key, category pii. Planted defect: columns requester_name, requester_email, requester_phone are personal data and the dataset open_support_data is readable by allUsers. Location: the Access section of dim_ticket_summary's plan.

PASS only if the review names this defect and ties it to that part of the plan. Generic advice with no tie to this table or step does not count. FAIL otherwise.

3-rollbackllm

Answer key, category rollback. Planted defect: the Rollout changes the live table (CREATE OR REPLACE TABLE over the live table) with no snapshot, clone or restore step, so there is no way back. Location: the Rollout section (CREATE OR REPLACE TABLE over the live table) of dim_ticket_summary's plan.

PASS only if the review names this defect and ties it to that part of the plan. Generic advice with no tie to this table or step does not count. FAIL otherwise.

judge votes: PASS PASS PASS

Evidence (what the judge was shown; for an image, a description of what was sent)
I reviewed the plan and appended a `## Data review (2026-11-04)` section to it. The verdict is **NOT READY**.

I found no existing model, table or contract that already answers this need. The repo has no `contracts/` or `dbt/` directory, so I couldn't check downstream lineage.

I didn't dry-run any query because I had no `bq` access. So no cost figure in the review is measured.

**Blockers**
- **Public personal data:** `allUsers` can read `public_marketing`, and the column list includes `email`, `full_name` and `date_of_birth`. The contract also says `classification: internal`, which doesn't match.
- **Column mismatch:** those three columns aren't in the source schema or the model SELECT, which produces only `ds, subscriber_id, amount`.
- **Possible double counting:** `SUM(amount)` ignores `updated_at`. If the CRM export re-delivers or corrects rows, the sums are wrong. The plan doesn't say what duplicate rows mean.
- **No consumer or segment logic:** the plan names no consumer or decision, and despite the name there is no segmentation in the model.
- **Rollout and rollback:** `DROP TABLE` has no rollback. The CTAS has no `ds` filter, so `require_partition_filter` would reject it, and without the filter it would scan all 300 GB.

**Should-fix**
- `primary_key` is missing from the contract, and your config requires it.
- A MERGE rerun leaves stale rows for that `ds`, and no backfill path is written.
- The target table has no partitioning, clustering or `require_partition_filter`.
- There are no tests, and no source arrival time to check the 07:00 UTC freshness SLA against.
- Late-arriving rows aren't handled.
- Downstream consumers aren't listed.

There is also one nit: the `dim_` prefix doesn't fit a daily snapshot.

I only appended the review section and left the rest of the plan as is. The author needs to resolve the blockers first. If it helps, I can run `wraith:grilling` on the consumer and duplicate-row questions.

Evidence (what the judge was shown; for an image, a description of what was sent)
I've reviewed the plan and the verdict is **NOT READY**. I appended the full findings table as `## Data review (2026-11-04)` at the end of `plan.md` and left the rest of the plan alone.

I didn't run any `bq` dry-runs, so the review has no measured byte counts. The only size in the plan is "about 300 GB" for the whole source table. I also found no existing models or contracts that already do this job.

**Blockers (4):**
- **Public access to personal data:** `public_marketing` with readers `allUsers` exposes `email`, `full_name` and `date_of_birth` to anyone on the internet.
- **Wrong classification:** the contract says `classification: internal` for those same columns, and there is no erasure path for them.
- **Unsafe rollout:** step 1 is `DROP TABLE` with no backup, step 2 is an unfinished `AS SELECT ...`, and the only check is "row counts".
- **Reruns aren't safe:** `SUM(amount)` doubles the amounts if the CRM re-sends a file or sends several versions of a subscriber. The source has `updated_at`, but the plan doesn't use it to pick the latest row.

**Should fix (6):**
- **Purpose:** the plan never says who uses the table, what decision it supports, or what a "segment" is. The table has only `amount`.
- **Contract:** `primary_key` is missing, though `AGENTS.md` requires it, and no contract file is planned.
- **Column mismatch:** the model SELECT omits the three personal columns that the Access section lists.
- **Stale rows:** the MERGE never deletes rows that disappear from the source on a rerun, and no backfill path is written.
- **Target table and cost:** the target table has no partitioning, clustering or `require_partition_filter`. The MERGE condition `d.ds = n.ds` also doesn't limit the scan of the target table.
- **Quality and late data:** there are no tests, no check of the 07:00 UTC freshness SLA, and no rule for rows that arrive after their `ds`.

Two nits cover downstream lineage and stating the `ds` snapshot model outright.

Next, I'd suggest you decide on the access model and the meaning of "segment" first. Both change the design, so I'd settle them before I edit anything.

- **Wrong classification:** the contract says `classification: internal` for those same columns, and there is no erasure path for them.
- **Unsafe rollout:** step 1 is `DROP TABLE` with no backup, step 2 is an unfinished `AS SELECT ...`, and the only check is "row counts".
- **Reruns aren't safe:** `SUM(amount)` doubles the amounts if the CRM re-sends a file or sends several versions of a subscriber. The source has `updated_at`, but the plan doesn't use it to pick the latest row.

**Should fix (6):**
- **Purpose:** the plan never says who uses the table, what decision it supports, or what a "segment" is. The table has only `amount`.
- **Contract:** `primary_key` is missing, though `AGENTS.md` requires it, and no contract file is planned.
- **Column mismatch:** the model SELECT omits the three personal columns that the Access section lists.
- **Stale rows:** the MERGE never deletes rows that disappear from the source on a rerun, and no backfill path is written.
- **Target table and cost:** the target table has no partitioning, clustering or `require_partition_filter`. The MERGE condition `d.ds = n.ds` also doesn't limit the scan of the target table.
- **Quality and late data:** there are no tests, no check of the 07:00 UTC freshness SLA, and no rule for rows that arrive after their `ds`.

Two nits cover downstream lineage and stating the `ds` snapshot model outright.

Next, I'd suggest you decide on the access model and the meaning of "segment" first. Both change the design, so I'd settle them before I edit anything.

I did not run the dry-run or `dbt ls`. `raw_crm.subscribers` and the dbt project aren't in the repo, and I didn't query the dev project. The cost and downstream-impact findings therefore come from reading the plan, not from measured bytes.

I searched the repo for an existing segments or subscribers model, table or contract and found none, so the plan doesn't duplicate anything.

**Blockers**
- **Public personal data:** `allUsers` can read `public_marketing`, and the column list includes `email`, `full_name` and `date_of_birth`. The contract says `internal`. Use a restricted dataset, reclassify, minimise the columns and add policy tags plus an erasure path.
- **Columns don't match:** the model SQL emits only `ds, subscriber_id, amount`. Nothing explains where the three personal-data columns come from.
- **Possible double counting:** `updated_at` suggests the same subscriber can appear several times in one `ds`, and `SUM(amount)` would add them up. The plan has to say what a source row is. If rows are versions, take the latest `updated_at` per subscriber.
- **Unsafe rollout:** `DROP TABLE` first has no rollback and leaves a window with no table. Build a new table and swap it in, and write the rollback.
- **No consumer:** the plan names no consumer, no decision the table feeds, and no cost of wrong, late or down.

**Should-fix**
- The contract lacks `primary_key`, which `contract_required_fields` requires, and no contract file is planned.
- The MERGE never removes subscribers who vanish from the source, and there is no backfill path. Replace it with a partition-scoped replace of `ds`.
- The target table has no partitioning, clustering or `require_partition_filter`. The MERGE has no `ds` predicate on the target, so it can scan the whole table.
- There are no tests, no schedule or alert for the 07:00 UTC SLA, and no row-count threshold.
- Late-arriving rows and downstream consumers aren't addressed.

The plan is **NOT READY**. I appended the full review as `## Data review (2026-11-04)` to `plan.md`. I changed nothing else in the file.

I didn't run any `bq` dry-runs, so the review states no byte costs. The only size figure is the plan's own "about 300 GB" for the whole source table. I didn't run the vendor skills either.

No existing model, table or contract in the repo covers this, so the premise check found nothing to block on. There are seven blockers:

1. **Personal data is public.** `public_marketing` is readable by `allUsers` and exposes `email`, `full_name` and `date_of_birth`. Fix this first.
2. **The contract is wrong.** It classifies the table as `internal` and has no `primary_key`, which your config requires. No contract file is planned.
3. **The consumer is unnamed and "segment" is undefined.** The model only sums `amount` per subscriber.
4. **The columns don't match.** The Access list has `email`, `full_name` and `date_of_birth`, but neither the source DDL nor the model has them.
5. **`SUM(amount)` can double-count.** A re-delivered or corrected file would be summed twice, and `updated_at` is ignored.
6. **The backfill breaks.** `CREATE TABLE ... AS SELECT` has no `ds` filter, so it fails on `require_partition_filter` or scans all 300 GB. That is far over the 10 GB dev limit.
7. **There is no rollback.** `DROP TABLE` has no backup and can't be rerun safely.

There are also four "should" findings and one nit:
- The MERGE never deletes rows that vanish from a corrected file.
- The target table has no DDL or cost estimate.
- There are no tests.
- Downstream consumers aren't listed.
- Late-arriving data isn't handled.

The first blocker needs your decision: who should read this table, and does anyone need `date_of_birth`? I can draft the plan edits for the other blockers once you answer.

judge votes: PASS PASS PASS

Judge votes✓ ✓ ✓
Evidence (what the judge was shown; for an image, a description of what was sent)
The plan is **not ready to build**. I appended a `## Data review (2026-11-04)` section to `plan.md` with 5 blockers, 7 should-fix items and 1 nit. I didn't change the rest of the plan.

**Blockers**
1. **Public personal data.** Access grants `allUsers` on `public_marketing`, and the columns include `email`, `full_name` and `date_of_birth`. The contract also labels the table `classification: internal`. Both need fixing, along with the readers and the erasure path.
2. **Destructive rollout.** It runs `DROP TABLE` and then `CREATE TABLE AS SELECT ...`. If the second step fails, the table is gone and nothing says how to get it back. The `SELECT` itself is left as `...`.
3. **Columns don't match.** The model outputs `ds, subscriber_id, amount`. Access lists `email`, `full_name` and `date_of_birth`, which no step produces. The source also has `updated_at`, so it may hold several versions per subscriber per `ds`, and `SUM(amount)` could double count.
4. **No consumer or decision.** The plan never defines what a "segment" is, and the model has no segment logic.

**Should-fix items**
- **No contract file, and `primary_key` is missing.** The contract has no file in `contracts/`, and `primary_key` is a required field.
- **Reruns aren't idempotent.** The MERGE never deletes subscribers who drop out of a `ds`, and there is no backfill path.
- **Target table is undeclared.** It has no partition, cluster or `require_partition_filter`, and the MERGE has no `d.ds = @ds` filter, so it scans the whole target each run.
- **Smaller gaps.** The plan has no tests, no source-arrival time for the 07:00 UTC SLA, no downstream list and no late-arriving-data rule. It also doesn't state the `ds` snapshot model, and it doesn't say what the final row-count check compares against.

I didn't dry-run any query because I have no `bq` access in this session. The cost finding rests only on the plan's own numbers (300 GB total, per-`ds` size unknown). I also found no existing model or contract in the repo that already covers this.

Decision for you: should the table carry PII at all? The model doesn't need it, and dropping the PII columns would resolve most of the access and classification findings. Once you've decided that, I can help revise the plan.

Run 1 66.7% 2 turns · $0.04 · 12:20:07 UTC
✓ pass 1-pii

judge votes: PASS PASS PASS

Judge votes✓ ✓ ✓
Evidence (what the judge was shown; for an image, a description of what was sent)
I read the plan. I wouldn't build it as written, mainly because of the first two issues below.

**Blockers**

1. **PII is exposed to everyone (Access).** `public_marketing` with `allUsers` readers makes the table readable by anyone on the internet. The column list includes `email`, `full_name` and `date_of_birth`. That also contradicts `classification: internal` in the contract. Fix it by restricting readers to a specific group. Drop `date_of_birth` unless there's a stated need. Consider hashing or omitting `email` and `full_name`. Reclassify the table as confidential or PII.
2. **The rollout is destructive (Rollout).** Dropping the table before recreating it causes an outage and risks missing the 07:00 UTC SLA. If step 2 fails there's no rollback. Instead, build into a new table or a staging name, validate it, then swap it in with `CREATE OR REPLACE` or a rename. "Check row counts" also needs concrete checks, such as comparing counts to the source, checking for duplicate keys, and checking for null keys.

**Correctness**

3. **The model and the access list don't match.** The model only produces `ds`, `subscriber_id` and `amount`. `email`, `full_name` and `date_of_birth` aren't in the source DDL or the SELECT. Nothing in the plan defines a "segment" either, even though the table is named `dim_newsletter_segments`.
4. **Duplicates may be double-counted.** The source has `updated_at`, and the delivery is described as "one file per run". Re-delivered or corrected rows for the same `(ds, subscriber_id)` would be inflated by `SUM(amount)`. Decide whether to sum rows or take the latest row per key (`QUALIFY ROW_NUMBER() OVER (PARTITION BY subscriber_id ORDER BY updated_at DESC) = 1`). Also state what `amount` means.
5. **Reruns leave stale rows.** The `MERGE` has no `WHEN NOT MATCHED BY SOURCE` clause. If a subscriber disappears from a re-delivered `ds`, the old row stays. Add a delete clause scoped to that `ds`.
6. **The target table is undefined.** The plan gives no partitioning or clustering for `dim_newsletter_segments`. Without `PARTITION BY ds` and a partition filter on `d.ds`, each `MERGE` scans the whole target. The source's `require_partition_filter` is respected (`ds = @ds`), so that part is fine.

**Smaller gaps**

- The contract has no schedule or dependency on the CRM export's arrival, so the 07:00 UTC SLA can't be checked. There are no tests or alerting.
- The plan is a draft dated 2026-10-12 and is about three weeks old. Confirm it's still current.

I'd have the data team fix items 1 to 3 first, since they change the schema and access model. Then I can build once the rollout is rewritten. Do you want me to draft a revised plan?

Judge votes✗ ✗ ✗
Evidence (what the judge was shown; for an image, a description of what was sent)
I read the plan. I wouldn't build it as written, mainly because of the first two issues below.

**Blockers**

1. **PII is exposed to everyone (Access).** `public_marketing` with `allUsers` readers makes the table readable by anyone on the internet. The column list includes `email`, `full_name` and `date_of_birth`. That also contradicts `classification: internal` in the contract. Fix it by restricting readers to a specific group. Drop `date_of_birth` unless there's a stated need. Consider hashing or omitting `email` and `full_name`. Reclassify the table as confidential or PII.
2. **The rollout is destructive (Rollout).** Dropping the table before recreating it causes an outage and risks missing the 07:00 UTC SLA. If step 2 fails there's no rollback. Instead, build into a new table or a staging name, validate it, then swap it in with `CREATE OR REPLACE` or a rename. "Check row counts" also needs concrete checks, such as comparing counts to the source, checking for duplicate keys, and checking for null keys.

**Correctness**

3. **The model and the access list don't match.** The model only produces `ds`, `subscriber_id` and `amount`. `email`, `full_name` and `date_of_birth` aren't in the source DDL or the SELECT. Nothing in the plan defines a "segment" either, even though the table is named `dim_newsletter_segments`.
4. **Duplicates may be double-counted.** The source has `updated_at`, and the delivery is described as "one file per run". Re-delivered or corrected rows for the same `(ds, subscriber_id)` would be inflated by `SUM(amount)`. Decide whether to sum rows or take the latest row per key (`QUALIFY ROW_NUMBER() OVER (PARTITION BY subscriber_id ORDER BY updated_at DESC) = 1`). Also state what `amount` means.
5. **Reruns leave stale rows.** The `MERGE` has no `WHEN NOT MATCHED BY SOURCE` clause. If a subscriber disappears from a re-delivered `ds`, the old row stays. Add a delete clause scoped to that `ds`.
6. **The target table is undefined.** The plan gives no partitioning or clustering for `dim_newsletter_segments`. Without `PARTITION BY ds` and a partition filter on `d.ds`, each `MERGE` scans the whole target. The source's `require_partition_filter` is respected (`ds = @ds`), so that part is fine.

**Smaller gaps**

- The contract has no schedule or dependency on the CRM export's arrival, so the 07:00 UTC SLA can't be checked. There are no tests or alerting.
- The plan is a draft dated 2026-10-12 and is about three weeks old. Confirm it's still current.

I'd have the data team fix items 1 to 3 first, since they change the schema and access model. Then I can build once the rollout is rewritten. Do you want me to draft a revised plan?

judge votes: PASS PASS PASS

Judge votes✓ ✓ ✓
Evidence (what the judge was shown; for an image, a description of what was sent)
I read the plan. I wouldn't build it as written. The first two findings are blockers.

**Blockers**

1. **The access setting exposes PII to everyone.** `public_marketing` with readers `allUsers` makes the table readable by anyone on the internet. The columns include `email`, `full_name` and `date_of_birth`. The contract also says `classification: internal`, which contradicts this. Restrict readers to a specific group. Consider dropping `date_of_birth` entirely, or moving the PII columns to a restricted table or a policy-tagged column.
2. **The PII columns have no source.** `raw_crm.subscribers` has only `ds, updated_at, subscriber_id, amount`. The model query selects only `ds, subscriber_id, amount`. So `email`, `full_name` and `date_of_birth` come from nowhere. Either a source is missing from the plan, or the Access section is wrong.
3. **Rollout step 1 is a bare `DROP TABLE`.** There's no backup, and the plan never shows the CTAS in full (`SELECT ...`). If step 2 fails, the table is gone and its readers break. Use `CREATE OR REPLACE TABLE`, or build the new table and then swap. Add a rollback path.

**Correctness**

4. **The model has no segments.** The goal is a segments table, but nothing defines a segment. The only measure is a summed `amount`. The `dim_` prefix is also odd for a table with one row per subscriber per `ds`, which is a snapshot.
5. **`SUM(amount)` can double-count.** The source is one CRM export file per run, and the table has `updated_at`. Re-exports or late corrections would create several rows per subscriber per `ds`, and the sum would inflate. Decide the dedupe rule, for example the latest `updated_at` per `subscriber_id`. Also state why a subscriber would ever have several rows.
6. **The target DDL is missing.** `dim_newsletter_segments` has no stated schema, partitioning or clustering. Without `PARTITION BY ds` and `CLUSTER BY subscriber_id`, the MERGE `ON` clause scans the whole target on every run. Add `d.ds = @ds` to the match condition so it can prune.
7. **No cleanup of subscribers who drop out.** The MERGE has no `WHEN NOT MATCHED BY SOURCE` clause. A rerun of a given `ds` won't remove subscribers who disappeared from the export. If the table is only ever loaded for one `ds` at a time, it could be simpler to overwrite the partition.

**Gaps**

8. **There's no schedule or backfill.** `freshness_sla: 07:00 UTC` has no scheduler or dependency on the CRM export arriving. `@ds` handles one day, and history isn't covered.
9. **Validation is too thin.** "Check row counts" doesn't catch the real risks. Add a uniqueness check on `(ds, subscriber_id)`, null checks, and a comparison of totals against the source.
10. **The contract is incomplete.** It has no column descriptions and no PII tagging. It also needs a classification that matches the data actually in the table.

I'd fix items 1–3 and define what a segment is (item 4) before building anything. Want me to draft a revised plan?

Run 3 66.7% 2 turns · $0.05 · 12:21:28 UTC
✓ pass 1-pii

judge votes: PASS PASS PASS

Judge votes✓ ✓ ✓
Evidence (what the judge was shown; for an image, a description of what was sent)
I found three blockers and several smaller issues in the plan.

## Blockers

1. **Access exposes PII to everyone** (Access section). `allUsers` makes `public_marketing` readable by anyone on the internet. The column list includes `email`, `full_name` and `date_of_birth`, and the Contract section classifies the table as `internal`. Restrict readers to a specific group, and drop or mask the PII columns unless a consumer needs them. Then make the classification match what the table holds.
2. **The rollout drops the table before the replacement exists** (Rollout, steps 1–2). If the `CREATE` fails or is slow, consumers have no table and there is nothing to roll back to. Build into a new table, or use `CREATE OR REPLACE`, and validate before swapping. Keep the old table until validation passes.
3. **The schema doesn't match the source** (Access vs Source). The source has `ds, updated_at, subscriber_id, amount`. The model selects `ds, subscriber_id, amount`. The access list adds `email`, `full_name` and `date_of_birth`, and the plan doesn't say where they come from. The plan also never defines the target table's DDL, partitioning or clustering.

## Correctness

- **Double counting on re-delivery.** The source is "one file per run", so a re-export or late correction can put two versions of a subscriber on the same `ds`. `SUM(amount)` then double-counts them, and `updated_at` is never used. Decide whether `amount` is additive or a snapshot. If it's a snapshot, keep the latest row per `(ds, subscriber_id)` by `updated_at` instead of summing.
- **The `MERGE` never deletes rows.** If a re-run produces fewer subscribers for a `ds`, the old rows stay. Add `WHEN NOT MATCHED BY SOURCE AND d.ds = @ds THEN DELETE`, or replace the whole partition.
- **The `MERGE` has no partition filter on the target.** `d.ds = n.ds` may not prune partitions, so the target could be fully scanned on every run. Add an explicit `d.ds = @ds` predicate. Partition and cluster the target the same way as the source.
- **NULL and duplicate keys.** A NULL `subscriber_id` would group into one row and never match in the `MERGE`. Add a uniqueness and not-null check.

## Smaller issues

- **The name doesn't match the content.** `dim_newsletter_segments` has no segment column. The grain is a per-subscriber daily amount, which looks more like a fact table. Either add the segment logic or rename it.
- **"Check row counts" is too vague.** State what to compare (source distinct subscribers per `ds` against target rows), the tolerance, and who signs off.
- **The `SELECT ...` in the `CREATE` is elided.** Write out the full statement and the backfill range. The 300 GB figure is ambiguous between the whole table and one `ds`. Give a cost estimate for the backfill, since `require_partition_filter` forces per-partition scans.
- **The freshness SLA has no dependency.** A 07:00 UTC SLA needs a schedule and a check that the CRM export has arrived. The plan has neither, and no alerting for a late or missing file.

I'd settle the access, schema and rollout points before building. I can draft a revised plan if you'd like.

Judge votes✓ ✓ ✓
Evidence (what the judge was shown; for an image, a description of what was sent)
I read the plan. I wouldn't build it as written. Findings, most serious first:

**Blockers**
1. **PII is exposed to everyone (Access, plan.md:44-45).** `public_marketing` with `allUsers` readers would publish `email`, `full_name` and `date_of_birth` to anyone. It also contradicts `classification: internal` in the contract. Restrict readers to a named group, drop or hash the PII columns, and make the classification match.
2. **The access columns don't match the model.** `email`, `full_name` and `date_of_birth` aren't in the source DDL (plan.md:14) or the model SELECT (plan.md:26). Either the plan is missing a join or the column list is wrong. Neither the model nor the source has a segment column either, despite the table's name.
3. **The rollout is destructive (plan.md:49-51).** `DROP TABLE` followed by `CREATE ... AS SELECT ...` leaves a window with no table, and there's no backup or rollback. The `SELECT ...` is elided. If it has no `ds` filter, it will be rejected because the source has `require_partition_filter = TRUE`. If it backfills every partition, it scans about 300 GB. Use `CREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS` plus the MERGE, or build a new table and swap it in. Add a snapshot first.

**Correctness**
4. **Reruns can double count.** Delivery is "one file per run" and the source has `updated_at`. If a `ds` is redelivered or corrected, `SUM(amount)` adds the duplicate rows. The MERGE masks this by overwriting, but the aggregate is still wrong. Decide whether rows are additive or versioned. If they're versioned, dedupe on the latest `updated_at` per `subscriber_id` instead of summing.
5. **The MERGE doesn't remove stale rows.** A subscriber who disappears from a re-delivered `ds` stays in the target. Add `WHEN NOT MATCHED BY SOURCE AND d.ds = @ds THEN DELETE`.
6. **The MERGE scans the whole target.** The ON clause doesn't prune partitions. Add `d.ds = @ds` to the ON clause, and partition the target by `ds` and cluster it by `subscriber_id`. The plan doesn't define the target DDL at all.

**Smaller items**
- "Check row counts" isn't a real validation. Add checks for unique `(ds, subscriber_id)`, non-null keys, and totals against the source for that `ds`.
- `freshness_sla: 07:00 UTC` has no scheduler, upstream dependency or alerting behind it.
- The contract has an owner but no description of columns or PII handling.

I'd fix items 1-3 before any build work. Items 4-6 need a decision from the data team about the source semantics. Do you want me to draft a revised plan?

6. **The MERGE scans the whole target.** The ON clause doesn't prune partitions. Add `d.ds = @ds` to the ON clause, and partition the target by `ds` and cluster it by `subscriber_id`. The plan doesn't define the target DDL at all.

**Smaller items**
- "Check row counts" isn't a real validation. Add checks for unique `(ds, subscriber_id)`, non-null keys, and totals against the source for that `ds`.
- `freshness_sla: 07:00 UTC` has no scheduler, upstream dependency or alerting behind it.
- The contract has an owner but no description of columns or PII handling.

I'd fix items 1-3 before any build work. Items 4-6 need a decision from the data team about the source semantics. Do you want me to draft a revised plan?
Run 5 66.7% 2 turns · $0.04 · 12:22:48 UTC
✓ pass 1-pii

judge votes: PASS PASS PASS

Judge votes✓ ✓ ✓
Evidence (what the judge was shown; for an image, a description of what was sent)
I read the plan and I'd fix a few things before we build it. They're in rough priority order.

**Blockers**

1. **The access section would publish PII.** `Readers: allUsers` makes the dataset public to anyone on the internet. The column list includes `email`, `full_name` and `date_of_birth`. The contract says `classification: internal`, so the plan contradicts itself. Restrict readers to a specific group, drop or hash the PII columns, and set the classification to match what the table holds.
2. **The rollout is destructive and has no fallback.** Step 1 is `DROP TABLE dim_newsletter_segments`. That leaves a window with no table, which puts the 07:00 UTC SLA at risk, and it gives no way back. If the table already exists, build into a new table or use `CREATE OR REPLACE`. Then validate it and swap it in. Step 2 is also a literal `SELECT ...`, so the plan doesn't say what the CTAS runs.
3. **The schema doesn't match the model.** The source table has `ds, updated_at, subscriber_id, amount`. It has no `email`, `full_name` or `date_of_birth`, and the model query doesn't select them. Either the access list is wrong, or the plan is missing a join to another source. Also, nothing in the plan computes a "segment", although the table is named for one.

**Correctness**

4. **Re-delivered exports will inflate amounts.** The source is one CRM file per run, and `updated_at` exists, but the model ignores it. If a file is re-sent or corrected, `SUM(amount)` double-counts. Decide whether rows are additive or versioned. If they're versioned, keep the latest row per `subscriber_id` by `updated_at` before aggregating.
5. **The MERGE can leave stale rows.** If a subscriber disappears from a re-run of the same `ds`, the old row stays. Add `WHEN NOT MATCHED BY SOURCE AND d.ds = @ds THEN DELETE`. Alternatively, overwrite the whole `ds` partition, which is simpler and idempotent.
6. **The MERGE may scan the whole target.** `d.ds = n.ds` is a join condition, not a pruning filter. Partition the target by `ds` and cluster it by `subscriber_id`. Add an explicit `d.ds = @ds` to the `ON` clause so BigQuery can prune.

**Smaller items**

7. **The table name and grain don't agree.** The grain is one row per subscriber per `ds`, which makes this a daily snapshot, not a `dim_` table.
8. **The checks are too thin.** "Check row counts" doesn't say against what. Add checks for uniqueness on `(ds, subscriber_id)`, null keys, and a comparison against the source `COUNT(DISTINCT subscriber_id)`.
9. **The contract is incomplete.** It has no column schema, PII tags or retention. The 07:00 UTC freshness SLA also doesn't say when the CRM export lands.

The partition filter on the 300 GB source is correct. `require_partition_filter` is satisfied, so the cost is one day's partition.

Items 1–3 need answers before we build. I can draft a revised plan with these changes if you want.

✓ pass 1-rollback

judge votes: PASS PASS PASS

Judge votes✓ ✓ ✓
Evidence (what the judge was shown; for an image, a description of what was sent)
I reviewed the plan and appended a `## Data review (2026-11-04)` section to `plan.md`. The verdict is **NOT READY**. I didn't edit the rest of the plan, so the fixes are yours to make.

I didn't run a dry-run. The plan gives no table for `snap_inventory`, and its own source DDL has no `warehouse_id`, so the queries wouldn't compile. The cost finding therefore says what's missing rather than quoting bytes.

I searched the repo for `snap_inventory`, `stock_levels` and "inventory". The plan is the only hit, so nothing already answers this. The repo has no dbt models or contracts yet.

**Blockers**
- **Grain mismatch:** the grain says "per sku per warehouse per ds". The Model SQL groups by `ds, sku` only. The Load SQL groups by `warehouse_id`, which the source DDL doesn't have. `primary_key` also leaves out `ds`.
- **Possible double counting:** the source has `counted_at`, so a sku can have several counts per `ds`. `SUM(amount)` would add repeated counts of the same stock. The plan needs to say whether rows are counts or deltas. If they're counts, take the latest per `ds, sku, warehouse_id` instead of summing.
- **Rollout and rollback:** `TRUNCATE` then `INSERT` can't be rerun per `ds`. It leaves an empty table if step 2 fails, step 2's `SELECT ...` is never written out, and there's no rollback.
- **Contract:** it's only a bullet list, not an ODCS file in `contracts/`. It's missing `freshness_sla`, which your AGENTS.md requires, and the change isn't classified as additive or breaking.

**Should-fix**
- **Consumer:** no consumer or decision is named, and there's no cost of wrong, late or down.
- **Stale rows on rerun:** the MERGE never deletes rows that vanish from the source for a `ds`.
- **Target table:** there's no DDL for partitioning, clustering or `require_partition_filter`. The MERGE also needs `d.ds = @ds` in its ON clause.
- **Cost:** bytes per `ds` and runs per day aren't given.
- **Hashing and access:** the plan says identifiers are hashed with SHA256, but no SQL does it. Unsalted hashes of low-entropy IDs are guessable, and hashing would break joins on `sku`. The dataset is named `snap_private` while the classification is `internal`.
- **Quality, lineage, late data:** there are no tests, no downstream consumers listed, and no rule for late-arriving data.

**Nits**
- Say outright that a `ds` snapshot is used and SCD2 isn't needed.
- Name the service account that runs the load.

Should I start fixing these? The grain question and the counts-versus-deltas question are the ones I'd settle first, since the SQL, contract and tests all follow from them.

I reviewed the plan and appended a `## Data review (2026-11-04)` section to `plan.md`. The verdict is **NOT READY**. I didn't run any `bq` dry-runs or `dbt ls`, because the repo has no dbt project, contract or source table to run them against. That means the plan's cost has no measured byte figure yet.

**Premise check:** Nothing else in the repo covers inventory snapshots, so the plan isn't duplicating an existing model.

**Blockers:**
- **Grain:** The plan says one row per sku per warehouse per ds. The Model query groups by `ds, sku` only. The Load query groups by `warehouse_id`, which doesn't exist in the source table definition.
- **Double counting:** The source has `counted_at`, so a sku may have several counts in one ds. `SUM(amount)` would then overstate stock. The plan should take the latest count instead, or say each ds holds only one.
- **Contract:** It has no `freshness_sla`, which `AGENTS.md` requires. It doesn't say whether the change is additive or breaking. Its primary key leaves out `ds`.

**Should-fix:**
- **Consumer:** No consumer or decision is named.
- **Backfill and reruns:** The backfill path isn't written. A rerun of a ds never removes keys that have disappeared from the source.
- **Target table and cost:**
  - The target table's partitioning, clustering and `require_partition_filter` aren't stated.
  - The MERGE's ON clause has no `d.ds = @ds` filter, so it scans the whole target.
  - The cost isn't estimated.
- **Classification:** The dataset is `snap_private` and the plan says identifiers are hashed, but the contract says `internal`. No query does the hashing. Unsalted SHA256 on a sku isn't real protection.
- **Quality:** There are no tests, freshness check or row-count threshold.
- **Late data:** Late-arriving rows aren't handled.

There are also two nits: state "ds snapshot, no SCD2", and list downstream consumers.

judge votes: PASS PASS PASS

Judge votes✓ ✓ ✓
Evidence (what the judge was shown; for an image, a description of what was sent)
I reviewed the plan and appended a `## Data review (2026-11-04)` section to it. The verdict is **NOT READY**. I didn't run any dry-runs because I have no `bq` access here, so the cost estimate is still open.

I found no existing model, table or contract that already covers this, so the idea itself is fine.

**Blockers**
1. **Grain:** The plan says one row per sku per warehouse per ds. The source table has no `warehouse_id` column, the Model query groups by `ds, sku` only, and the Load query groups by `warehouse_id` as well.
2. **Summing counts:** The source has `counted_at`, so a sku can have several counts in one ds. `SUM(amount)` then overstates stock, unless each row is a delta. The plan needs to say which it is.
3. **Contract:** There is no `freshness_sla`, which your `contract_required_fields` setting requires. There is also no contract file in `contracts/` and no additive-or-breaking label.
4. **Rollout:** `TRUNCATE` followed by `INSERT` leaves the table empty if the insert fails. The plan has no rollback and no backfill path. I recommend a per-ds partition overwrite instead.

**Should-fix**
- The MERGE never removes rows that disappeared from the source on a rerun.
- The contract's primary key leaves out `ds`.
- The `snap_inventory` DDL is missing: partitioning, clustering and `require_partition_filter`.
- The SHA256 hashing claim is unexplained. An unsalted hash of a sku is reversible by guessing, and the plan doesn't say whether any column is personal data.
- The dataset is called `snap_private` but the contract says `classification: internal`.
- There are no tests and no way to catch silently dropped rows.
- Nothing says how late-arriving rows are handled.

I reviewed the plan and appended a `## Data review (2026-11-04)` section to `plan.md`. The verdict is **NOT READY**, because of four blockers.

**Blockers**
1. **Grain and `warehouse_id` mismatch.** The grain says "per sku per warehouse per ds". The source DDL has no `warehouse_id`, the Model SQL leaves it out, and the Load SQL uses it.
2. **Possible double counting.** The source has `counted_at`, so a sku can be counted several times in one `ds`, and `SUM(amount)` would add those counts together. The plan needs a rule, such as taking the latest count.
3. **Rollout.** `TRUNCATE` followed by `INSERT` is not atomic, and the `SELECT` is elided. The table is new, so the truncate isn't needed. The plan also has no backfill range and no rollback.
4. **Contract.** The project requires `freshness_sla` (set in `AGENTS.md`), and the contract has none. It also has no schema or quality rules, and `primary_key` leaves out `ds`.

**Should-fix**
- No consumer or decision is named.
- A `MERGE` rerun leaves stale rows behind, so replace the whole `ds` partition instead.
- There is no DDL for `snap_inventory`: partitioning, `require_partition_filter` and a pruning filter on the `MERGE` target are all missing.
- The "hashed with SHA256" line doesn't match the model. Stock levels don't look like personal data, a plain SHA256 of a `sku` is reversible, and the dataset is called `snap_private` while the classification is `internal`.
- There are no tests or freshness checks, and `ds` and late-arriving rows are undefined.

So the review has no cost number, and the cost finding asks the author to measure it. The "nothing existing serves this" check only searched this repo, not the warehouse.

**Blockers**
- **Grain doesn't match the SQL.** The grain says per sku per warehouse per ds. The Model SQL groups by `ds, sku` only, and the source DDL has no `warehouse_id` column, though the Load SQL selects it.
- **Possible double counting.** The source has `counted_at`, so a sku may have several counts in one ds. `SUM(amount)` would add repeated counts together.
- **Wrong primary key.** The contract key `sku, warehouse_id` leaves out `ds`, so it isn't unique for a daily snapshot.
- **Unsafe rollout.** `TRUNCATE` followed by `INSERT` leaves the table empty during the load. It isn't safe to rerun if step 2 fails. The plan has no rollback and no backfill path.
- **Incomplete contract.** `freshness_sla` is required by `AGENTS.md` and is missing. The plan doesn't classify the change as additive or breaking, and no contract file exists.

**Should-fix items**
- **Consumer.** No consumer or decision is named.
- **MERGE.** It never deletes rows that disappear from a re-exported ds, and it relies on column order. It also lacks a literal `d.ds = @ds` partition filter on the target.
- **Target table.** `snap_inventory` has no partition, cluster or `require_partition_filter` DDL.
- **Cost.** The cost is not estimated.
- **Hashing.** The plan claims SHA256 hashing, but no SQL does it. The dataset is named `snap_private` while the classification is `internal`.
- **Quality.** The plan has no tests and no freshness check.
- **Downstream.** It lists no downstream models or dashboards.
- **Late data.** It doesn't say how late-arriving data is handled.

Run 1 33.3% 2 turns · $0.05 · 12:28:48 UTC
✓ pass 1-rollback

judge votes: PASS PASS PASS

Judge votes✓ ✓ ✓
Evidence (what the judge was shown; for an image, a description of what was sent)
I read the plan. It has internal contradictions that should be fixed before we build.

**Blocking**

1. **`warehouse_id` isn't in the source.** The `raw_wms.stock_levels` DDL has `ds, counted_at, sku, amount` only. The grain, the MERGE and the contract all use `warehouse_id`. Either the DDL is incomplete or the column has to come from somewhere else. The query in the Model section also omits it, so it doesn't produce the stated grain.
2. **`SUM(amount)` may double-count.** The source has `counted_at`, so a sku can have several counts in one `ds`. If those are point-in-time stock levels, summing them is wrong. You'd want the latest count per sku and warehouse (`QUALIFY ROW_NUMBER() OVER (PARTITION BY sku, warehouse_id ORDER BY counted_at DESC) = 1`). The plan needs to say which it is.
3. **Hashing contradicts the rest of the plan.** Access says identifiers are hashed with SHA256, but no query does that, and the column list shows plain `sku`. If hashing is intended, unsalted SHA256 of SKUs is easy to reverse, and the hash has to be applied consistently in the MERGE key and the primary key. Decide whether hashing is needed at all. The `internal` classification and a dataset named `snap_private` don't make that clear.
4. **The rollout is destructive.** `TRUNCATE` followed by `INSERT` leaves the table empty if the insert fails, and readers see an empty table in between. The `SELECT ...` is also unspecified. Safer options are to build into a new table and swap it in, or to load per `ds` partition and leave the rest alone. Step 3, "check row counts," has no expected numbers or pass/fail criterion, and there's no rollback.

**Should fix**

5. **The primary key is missing `ds`.** The grain is sku, warehouse and `ds`, but the contract says `primary_key: sku, warehouse_id`. As written, that key is violated by the second day's load.
6. **The MERGE isn't fully idempotent.** If a sku and warehouse pair disappears from the source on a re-run, its stale row stays. Use `WHEN NOT MATCHED BY SOURCE AND d.ds = @ds THEN DELETE`, or replace the partition outright.
7. **The backfill is undefined.** `require_partition_filter = TRUE` means a backfill has to go one `ds` at a time. Each day scans a slice of the roughly 600 GB table. The plan doesn't say how far back to backfill, how late-arriving exports are handled, or what the schedule and freshness SLA are.
8. **The target table is under-specified.** It has no DDL (types, partitioning on `ds`, clustering, `require_partition_filter`). It also names no reader roles beyond the group, and no retention.

If you tell me whether the `warehouse_id` and hashing points are plan typos or real requirements, I can draft a corrected version of the plan.

Run 2 33.3% 2 turns · $0.04 · 12:29:50 UTC
✗ fail 1-rollback

judge votes: FAIL FAIL FAIL

Judge votes✗ ✗ ✗
Evidence (what the judge was shown; for an image, a description of what was sent)
I read the plan. It has several problems that I'd fix before building.

**Blocking: the plan contradicts itself**
1. **`warehouse_id` isn't in the source.** The `raw_wms.stock_levels` DDL (line 14) has `ds, counted_at, sku, amount` and no `warehouse_id`. The grain (line 23), the MERGE (lines 36–37), the access columns (line 45) and the contract key (line 56) all depend on it. Either the DDL is incomplete or the grain is wrong. Please confirm which.
2. **The Model query doesn't match the grain.** Lines 26–29 group by `ds, sku` only, while the grain says per sku per warehouse. The MERGE query on line 36 does include `warehouse_id`. Use one canonical query.
3. **`SUM(amount)` may double-count.** The source has `counted_at`, which suggests several counts per sku per day. If each count is a full stock level, summing them inflates the number. You'd want the latest `counted_at` per sku and warehouse, not a sum. Please confirm how the export is shaped.
4. **The contract key is missing `ds`.** `primary_key: sku, warehouse_id` (line 56) would make a daily snapshot unique per sku and warehouse across all dates. The MERGE joins on `ds` too, so the key should be `ds, sku, warehouse_id`.
5. **The hashing claim isn't implemented.** Line 45 says identifiers are hashed with SHA256, but no query does that. Either add the hashing, with a salt or key, since hashed SKUs are easy to reverse. Or drop the claim.

**Rollout (lines 49–51)**
- `TRUNCATE` followed by `INSERT ... SELECT ...` is destructive and not atomic. Readers see an empty table in between, and a failed insert leaves it empty. The `SELECT` is also never specified. Use `CREATE OR REPLACE TABLE ... AS` or the idempotent MERGE per `ds`.
- "Check row counts" has no baseline or threshold, and it comes after the destructive step. Add pre-checks, such as expected counts per `ds` and null or duplicate checks on the key.
- There's no backfill plan. The source is about 600 GB and `require_partition_filter` forces a partition filter, so history has to be loaded `ds` by `ds`. Please state the date range and the expected cost.

**Smaller gaps**
- The target table has no DDL: no types, no partitioning on `ds`, no clustering, and no `require_partition_filter`. A MERGE without a partition predicate on the target scans the whole table.
- The MERGE never removes rows when a sku and warehouse disappears from a re-delivered `ds`. Decide whether that matters.
- Nothing covers late or missing daily exports, or a re-delivery of the same `ds`. The MERGE handles re-runs, but there's no freshness check or alert.
- `classification: internal` sits oddly next to a dataset called `snap_private` that is restricted to one group. Check that these agree.
- Nothing covers nulls in `amount` or `sku`, or negative stock.

Once you settle items 1–3, I can update the plan with a consistent grain, query, key and rollout. Do you want me to do that?

judge votes: PASS PASS PASS

Judge votes✓ ✓ ✓
Evidence (what the judge was shown; for an image, a description of what was sent)
I found several problems in the plan. The first four would break the build or give wrong numbers, so I'd fix those before we build.

**Blocking**

1. **`warehouse_id` isn't in the source.** `raw_wms.stock_levels` has `(ds, counted_at, sku, amount)` (lines 13-16). The Load `MERGE` (line 36) selects and groups by `warehouse_id`, so it will fail. Either the source DDL is stale or the export needs a warehouse column. Please confirm which.
2. **The Model and Load disagree.** The Model query (lines 26-29) groups by `ds, sku` and has no warehouse. That contradicts the stated grain on line 23 and the `MERGE` on line 36. Pick one definition and use it in both places.
3. **`SUM(amount)` is probably wrong.** The source has `counted_at`, so a sku can be counted several times a day. Stock levels are point-in-time readings, so adding them double-counts. You likely want the latest count per sku and warehouse for the day, for example `QUALIFY ROW_NUMBER() OVER (PARTITION BY sku, warehouse_id ORDER BY counted_at DESC) = 1`. If each row is a delta, the plan should say so.
4. **The rollout is destructive and doesn't match the Load.**
   - `TRUNCATE` followed by `INSERT ... SELECT ...` (lines 49-50) isn't atomic. A failed insert leaves the table empty, and the plan has no rollback.
   - It uses a different mechanism from the `MERGE`, and the `SELECT ...` is never written out.
   - The source has `require_partition_filter = TRUE`, so a backfill has to loop over a `ds` range. A full read is about 600 GB, and the plan doesn't give a range or a cost estimate.
   - "Check row counts" has nothing to compare against and runs after the damage is done.
   - Suggested fix: create the table once, backfill with the same `MERGE` per `ds`, and validate against the source before readers switch over.

**Should fix**

5. **Hashing isn't implemented.** Access (line 45) says identifiers are hashed with SHA256, but no query does it. It also doesn't say which columns are hashed. If `sku` is hashed, joins against other tables break unless they hash it the same way. Salting is also unspecified.
6. **The contract's primary key is missing `ds`.** Line 56 gives `sku, warehouse_id`, but the grain and the `MERGE` key include `ds`. The key should be `ds, sku, warehouse_id`.
7. **The classification looks inconsistent.** The plan has `internal` on line 57 but puts the data in a dataset called `snap_private`. Check that this is intended.
8. **`snap_inventory` has no DDL.** The plan doesn't give types, partitioning on `ds`, clustering, or whether a partition filter is required. A `MERGE` on `ds` without partitioning scans the whole target.

**Minor**

- The `MERGE` never deletes rows that disappear from a re-delivered day. Say whether that's intended.
- The plan doesn't cover late or re-sent exports, or `NULL` amounts.
- The plan has no schedule or dependency on the export landing.

I can draft a corrected version of the plan if you tell me two things: where `warehouse_id` comes from, and whether `amount` is a level or a delta.

judge votes: PASS FAIL PASS

Judge votes✓ ✗ ✓
Evidence (what the judge was shown; for an image, a description of what was sent)
I read the plan and found problems that I'd fix before building. They're ordered by how badly they'd hurt.

**Blocking**

1. **`warehouse_id` is missing from the source.** The `CREATE TABLE` for `raw_wms.stock_levels` has only `ds, counted_at, sku, amount`. The Load query and the stated grain both use `warehouse_id`. Either the DDL is stale or the Load query would fail. Confirm the real schema.
2. **The Model and Load queries disagree.** The grain is sku × warehouse × ds, but the Model query groups by `ds, sku` only. This would merge warehouses together. The Load query is the one that matches the grain.
3. **The Rollout is destructive.** `TRUNCATE` followed by `INSERT` leaves the table empty if the insert fails, and readers see an empty table in between. The `SELECT ...` is also elided, so it's unclear what it loads. It also contradicts the idempotent `MERGE` in Load. Use the MERGE for daily runs. For a backfill, use `CREATE OR REPLACE` or a single transaction, and state the date range.
4. **The `SUM(amount)` may double-count.** The source has `counted_at`, which suggests several counts per sku per day. Stock levels are point-in-time readings, so summing them is wrong. You probably want the latest `counted_at` per sku and warehouse, then a sum across any finer grain. The plan doesn't say whether the export has one count per day or several.

**Should fix**

5. **The contract contradicts the grain.** `primary_key: sku, warehouse_id` leaves out `ds`, but the grain and the MERGE key both include it.
6. **Hashing is stated but not implemented.** Access says "Identifiers are hashed with SHA256", but no query hashes `sku` or `warehouse_id`. Hashing would also break joins to other tables unless it's consistent everywhere. Decide whether hashing is needed. Classification is `internal`, yet the dataset is named `snap_private`. Make those match.
7. **The target table has no DDL.** It needs a definition with `PARTITION BY ds`, clustering, and a `require_partition_filter` setting. Otherwise the MERGE scans the whole target every day. `INSERT ROW` also depends on the column order matching exactly.
8. **There's no handling for late or missing deliveries.** The export arrives once a day, but nothing checks that partition `@ds` exists before the load runs. Running against an empty partition would write nothing, or with a delete step it would wipe the day. Rows that disappear from a re-delivered partition also stay behind, because the MERGE has no `WHEN NOT MATCHED BY SOURCE`.
9. **"Check row counts" is too weak.** Add checks for key uniqueness, nulls in `sku` and `warehouse_id`, and that the total `amount` matches the source for that `ds`.

**Minor**

- Say which timezone `ds` and `counted_at` use.
- The author is "data team" and the owner is data-platform. Confirm that's intended.

Questions before I draft fixes:
- Does the real source table have `warehouse_id`?
- Can there be several counts per sku and warehouse per day?

Run 5 66.7% 2 turns · $0.05 · 12:32:27 UTC
✓ pass 1-rollback

judge votes: PASS PASS PASS

Judge votes✓ ✓ ✓
Evidence (what the judge was shown; for an image, a description of what was sent)
The plan isn't ready to build. The source schema, the model and the load disagree with each other, and the rollout is risky.

**Blocking**
1. **`warehouse_id` isn't in the source.** `raw_wms.stock_levels` has `(ds, counted_at, sku, amount)`. The grain, the MERGE and the access section all use `warehouse_id`, so the MERGE would fail as written. Either the export is missing the column (confirm with the WMS team) or the grain is wrong.
2. **The Model and Load queries disagree.** The Model query groups by `ds, sku` and the Load groups by `ds, sku, warehouse_id`. The Model query also contradicts the stated grain. Pick one definition.
3. **`SUM(amount)` is probably wrong.** The table has `counted_at`, so a sku can have several counts in one `ds`. Stock levels are point-in-time values, not additive, so summing them would double-count. You likely want the latest `counted_at` per sku (and warehouse) per `ds`, using `QUALIFY ROW_NUMBER() OVER (...) = 1` or `ARRAY_AGG(... ORDER BY counted_at DESC LIMIT 1)`. If they really are additive deltas, say so in the plan.
4. **The rollout is destructive and non-atomic.** `TRUNCATE` followed by `INSERT ... SELECT ...` leaves the table empty if the insert fails. The `SELECT` is also left elided.
   - A backfill must carry a `ds` range filter, because the source has `require_partition_filter = TRUE`. A 600 GB scan is also costly, so the plan should bound it.
   - "Check row counts" has no expected value to compare against, and there's no rollback.
   - Safer: build into a staging table, validate it, then swap with `CREATE OR REPLACE` or a partition-wise overwrite. Better still, skip the TRUNCATE and backfill with the idempotent MERGE per `ds`.

**Should fix**
5. **The primary key omits `ds`.** The contract says `sku, warehouse_id`, but the grain and the MERGE key include `ds`. It should be `ds, sku, warehouse_id`.
6. **The access section is inconsistent.** It says identifiers are hashed with SHA256, but the listed columns are raw and no query hashes anything. Hashing `sku` would also break joins to other tables. Decide which columns are hashed and where. The dataset is called `snap_private`, but the classification is `internal`. Make those match.
7. **The MERGE never deletes.** A rerun for a `ds` won't remove rows that disappeared upstream, such as a sku that was restated or dropped. Add `WHEN NOT MATCHED BY SOURCE AND d.ds = @ds THEN DELETE`, or delete the partition and reload it.
8. **There are no data-quality or freshness checks.** The source arrives once a day, but the plan doesn't say what happens when the export is late, partial or restated. Add a check that the `ds` partition exists and is non-empty before the load, plus uniqueness and null checks on the key.

**Minor**
- Define `snap_inventory`'s DDL, including partitioning on `ds`, clustering and `require_partition_filter`. The plan doesn't specify it, and the MERGE's `d.ds = n.ds` pruning depends on it.
- The plan is a draft dated 2026-10-12, so confirm it hasn't gone stale.

If you tell me what to do about items 1 to 3, I can draft a corrected plan.
✗ fail 2-contract

judge votes: FAIL FAIL FAIL

Judge votes✗ ✗ ✗
Evidence (what the judge was shown; for an image, a description of what was sent)
The plan isn't ready to build. The source schema, the model and the load disagree with each other, and the rollout is risky.

**Blocking**
1. **`warehouse_id` isn't in the source.** `raw_wms.stock_levels` has `(ds, counted_at, sku, amount)`. The grain, the MERGE and the access section all use `warehouse_id`, so the MERGE would fail as written. Either the export is missing the column (confirm with the WMS team) or the grain is wrong.
2. **The Model and Load queries disagree.** The Model query groups by `ds, sku` and the Load groups by `ds, sku, warehouse_id`. The Model query also contradicts the stated grain. Pick one definition.
3. **`SUM(amount)` is probably wrong.** The table has `counted_at`, so a sku can have several counts in one `ds`. Stock levels are point-in-time values, not additive, so summing them would double-count. You likely want the latest `counted_at` per sku (and warehouse) per `ds`, using `QUALIFY ROW_NUMBER() OVER (...) = 1` or `ARRAY_AGG(... ORDER BY counted_at DESC LIMIT 1)`. If they really are additive deltas, say so in the plan.
4. **The rollout is destructive and non-atomic.** `TRUNCATE` followed by `INSERT ... SELECT ...` leaves the table empty if the insert fails. The `SELECT` is also left elided.
   - A backfill must carry a `ds` range filter, because the source has `require_partition_filter = TRUE`. A 600 GB scan is also costly, so the plan should bound it.
   - "Check row counts" has no expected value to compare against, and there's no rollback.
   - Safer: build into a staging table, validate it, then swap with `CREATE OR REPLACE` or a partition-wise overwrite. Better still, skip the TRUNCATE and backfill with the idempotent MERGE per `ds`.

**Should fix**
5. **The primary key omits `ds`.** The contract says `sku, warehouse_id`, but the grain and the MERGE key include `ds`. It should be `ds, sku, warehouse_id`.
6. **The access section is inconsistent.** It says identifiers are hashed with SHA256, but the listed columns are raw and no query hashes anything. Hashing `sku` would also break joins to other tables. Decide which columns are hashed and where. The dataset is called `snap_private`, but the classification is `internal`. Make those match.
7. **The MERGE never deletes.** A rerun for a `ds` won't remove rows that disappeared upstream, such as a sku that was restated or dropped. Add `WHEN NOT MATCHED BY SOURCE AND d.ds = @ds THEN DELETE`, or delete the partition and reload it.
8. **There are no data-quality or freshness checks.** The source arrives once a day, but the plan doesn't say what happens when the export is late, partial or restated. Add a check that the `ds` partition exists and is non-empty before the load, plus uniqueness and null checks on the key.

**Minor**
- Define `snap_inventory`'s DDL, including partitioning on `ds`, clustering and `require_partition_filter`. The plan doesn't specify it, and the MERGE's `d.ds = n.ds` pruning depends on it.
- The plan is a draft dated 2026-10-12, so confirm it hasn't gone stale.

If you tell me what to do about items 1 to 3, I can draft a corrected plan.
